          Interview the user relentlessly until you reach a shared understanding. Map this as a **design tree**: every decision branches into the decisions that hang off it.
          
          Work the tree in **rounds**. The **frontier** is every decision whose prerequisites are already settled: the questions you can ask _now_ without guessing at answers you haven't heard yet. Ask the whole frontier in one round: number each question and give your recommended answer. Then wait for the user's answers before the next round.
          
          Format a round like so:
          
          ```
          ❓ **Q1** - **<question title>**: <question body, might be multiple paragraphs, including multiple choices>
          
          ➡️ <your recommended answer>
          
          ---
          
          ❓ **Q2** - **<question title>**: <question body, might be multiple paragraphs, including multiple choices>
          
          ➡️ <your recommended answer>
          ```
          
          Word each question so "yes" accepts your recommended answer.
          
          Each round the user answers reshapes the tree: settled decisions push the frontier outward and unblock questions that depended on them. Recompute the frontier and ask the next round. A question whose answer depends on another question still open in this round belongs to a _later_ round, not this one.
          
          Finding _facts_ is your job, never the user's. When a frontier question needs a fact from the environment (filesystem, tools, etc.), dispatch a sub-agent to find it; don't ask the user for anything you could look up yourself. Don't block on it: a running exploration is an unsettled prerequisite, so only the questions downstream of it wait for the sub-agent to report; ask the rest of the frontier now. The _decisions_ are the user's: put each to them and wait.
          
          The session is done when the frontier is empty: every branch of the design tree visited, nothing left silently assumed. Do not act on it until the user confirms you have reached a shared understanding.

⏺ I read the page. It's the assets half of a consolidated balance sheet (資産の部), in 千円, with two period columns. Rows are nested by indentation up to 4 levels. It has △ negatives, "－" nils (建設仮勘定 prior year, 長期貸付金 prior year) and a "△0" (貸倒引当金, current year). It also has repeated labels (その他 ×3, リース資産 ×2, 減価償却累計額 ×3) and (純額) net rows. The page has no liabilities or equity.
  
  I'll go one question at a time, as CLAUDE.md says.
  
  ❓ **Q1 - Shape of one output record**: What is the unit of output? I suggest a long (tidy) table with one record per line item per period, not a wide table with one row per label.
  
  Fields:
  - `path`: the section path as a list, e.g. `["資産の部","固定資産","有形固定資産","リース資産","減価償却累計額"]`
  - `label`: the label as printed
  - `role`: one of `header`, `item`, `contra`, `net`, `subtotal`, `total`
  - `period`: e.g. `2025-03-31`
  - `value`: int, or `None` for nil
  - `raw`: the original string, e.g. `△1,923,585`
  - `unit`: `thousand_yen`, so 千円 stays explicit and we never silently multiply
  - `warnings`: list
  
  A page-level `warnings` list sits beside the rows. Output is JSON as the source of truth, plus a CSV view.
  
  Why long: the same table then works for 2 columns or 5, English or Japanese, and the identity checks run per period. A wide table would need a different schema for each layout.
  
  ➡️ Yes: long records with those fields, JSON as the source of truth, CSV as a view. Values are stored in the printed unit (千円) with `unit` recorded, not converted to yen.

⏺ Q1 is recorded in `docs/decisions.md` as D1, including `parent` and `label_en`. `label_en` is a translation for you only and is never used in any check.
  
  `parent` needs one more decision, because the same label appears several times, so a label can't be the pointer.
  
  ❓ **Q2 - What `parent` means and how rows are identified**: Give every record a stable `id` of the form `<period>|<path joined by />`. That is unique even for repeated labels, because the path includes the section. A repeated label could still collide inside one parent, so `id` would need an ordinal suffix.
  
  `parent` then holds the `id` of one row:
  - For a contra line (減価償却累計額) and a net line (（純額）), `parent` is the gross item it belongs to, e.g. 建物及び構築物.
  - For a plain item, `parent` is the section header it sits under, e.g. 有形固定資産.
  - For a subtotal or total, `parent` is its section header (or `None` at the top).
  
  `parent` says what a row belongs to. Which rows sum into which subtotal is a separate job for the identity checks and is not stored in `parent`.
  
  ➡️ Yes. Use `id` = period + path + ordinal if needed, and `parent` as described. Contra and net rows point to their gross item, and everything else points to its enclosing header.

╔══ YOU ═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

║ things. The section is already in path, so parent should only be used for
║ the gross, contra and net link, and be empty for everything else. Second,
║ always put the row's position on the page in the id (a plain ordinal),
║ not only when there's a collision, so ids never change when a page gets
║ a new row or a repeated label.
╚═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

⏺ Edit(/Users/teja/Desktop/ur-ai-parser-setup/docs/decisions.md)

  ⎿  The file /Users/teja/Desktop/ur-ai-parser-setup/docs/decisions.md has been updated successfully. (file state is current in your context — no need to Read it back)

⏺ I've recorded D2 with your `parent` rule. The `id` part is still open, because your two goals conflict.
  
  A plain position ordinal changes when a row is added above it. If a later page version gains one row, every id below that row shifts. So "always a position ordinal" and "ids never change when a page gets a new row" can't both hold across versions. They are only consistent for one parse of one page.
  
  ❓ **Q3 - What the `id` is stable against**: There are two options.
  
  - **(a)** `id` = `<row ordinal>` plus `<period>`, e.g. `r17|2026-03-31`. It is simple and unique, and stable within one parse. It shifts if the page changes. A repeated label can never collide, since each row has its own ordinal. For `parent` links and for checking a parse against hand-written gold answers, this is enough.
  - **(b)** Keep the ordinal, and also store a separate `key` of `path + label + occurrence index among identical siblings`. This survives inserted rows and repeated labels, and is what you'd use to compare two versions of a page. It costs one more field.
  
  ➡️ (a) for now. The ordinal is the `id`, and `parent` points to it. We add (b) later only if we compare page versions, which this task doesn't need.

╔══ YOU ═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
║ Yes to (a), the ordinal as the id. One condition: the hand-written gold

⏺ I've recorded your condition in `docs/decisions.md` as D3. Gold answers are keyed by (path, label, period) and never by ordinal. A key that matches zero or several rows fails the test. The contra row's `path` must include its gross item so the repeated labels stay unique.
  
  Next is the main question you set: how we decide the parser is correct.
  
  ❓ **Q4 - What "correct" means and the headline measure**: I suggest three layers, each reported separately.
  
  1. **Cell exactness against gold.** For each hand-checked record, `value` must match exactly. That includes nil vs 0 (`－` is `None`, `△0` is 0) and the sign (`△1,234` is -1,234).
  2. **Structure against gold.** `path` and `role` must match, so a 減価償却累計額 sits under the right gross item.
  3. **Accounting identities, with no gold needed.** Items sum to their subtotal, gross plus accumulated depreciation equals net, and current plus fixed equals total assets. A difference within a few units is reported, not hidden.
  
  The headline number is the share of gold records matched exactly on layers 1 and 2. A wrong value with no warning counts as worse than a wrong value the parser flagged. The report states both counts, so a parser that flags what it doubts scores better than one that guesses.
  
  ➡️ Yes: three layers reported separately. The headline is exact match on gold, and silent errors are counted apart from flagged ones.

⏺ I've recorded both additions as D4. I checked the tolerance against this page by hand. The prior-year 流動資産 items sum to 5,863,681 against a printed 5,863,684 (difference 3). The current year sums to 3,377,405 against a printed 3,377,407 (difference 2). With 10 items the limit is 5, so both pass and the rule is workable.
  
  There is one edge. The subtotal is itself rounded, so strictly the bound is 0.5 per item plus 0.5. That matters only for an odd number of items, e.g. 3 items allows 1 under your rule and 2 under the strict one. I left that as an open point in D4.
  
  The bigger problem is the gold answers.
  
  ❓ **Q5 - How you write gold from the image without reading Japanese**: Gold is keyed by (path, label, period), and the labels are Japanese. You told me you can't read Japanese. You can read the numbers, `△` and `－` directly from the image. I suggest this workflow:
  
  1. I produce a blank gold template with one row per printed line, in page order. Each row has the Japanese label as printed, my `label_en`, and an empty `value`. Labels are copied only to name the row, not to supply values.
  2. You fill `value` for each chosen row, reading only the printed digits, `△` and `－` from the image. You don't need to read Japanese.
  3. A separate step checks that each gold row's label really is on that page line, so a mismatched label is caught. It does this without using the parser.
  
  The risk is that my labels and `label_en` come from the same text extraction. If the extraction garbled a label, the gold key would carry the same error. Step 3 would catch that.
  
  ➡️ Yes. I build the template, you fill the values from the image, and a check validates the labels. Do you also want a second pair of eyes on the 20 to 30 chosen rows (someone who reads Japanese), or is your read of the numbers enough?

  ❓ **Q7 - What the parser does when a check fails**: D4 says an identity difference beyond tolerance is a failure. I suggest the parser still returns every row exactly as read and never adjusts a number to make a sum work. It adds `warnings` on the rows involved, plus a page-level status:
  - `ok`: every identity holds within tolerance.
  - `failed`: at least one identity is beyond tolerance, listed with the expected sum, the printed value and the difference.
  - `unreadable`: a value couldn't be parsed. Its record has `value=None` and a warning, and the raw text is kept in `raw`.
  
  A downstream user then sees the data and knows which part not to trust. Nothing is dropped, and nothing is guessed.
  
  ➡️ Yes. Always output all rows as read, never adjust values, and carry the three-state page status and per-row warnings. A value that can't be parsed gets `None` plus a warning, kept apart from a real nil. For that, record whether a `None` is a printed nil (`－`) or a parse failure.

  Routing on a failed check raises a question. A failed identity doesn't always mean the text parser is wrong. The filing itself may print numbers that don't sum, and then a vision model fails the same check. A vision model could also produce numbers that satisfy the sums while being wrong. Passing identities is a signal, not proof, which is why gold stays the real measure.
  
  ❓ **Q9 - Which output wins when both extractors run**: I suggest the following.
  - Return the output with the better check result. Fewer failed identities wins, and any `unreadable` counts against it. On a tie, the text output wins.
  - Never merge cell by cell. Each output is internally consistent or it isn't.
  - Keep both outputs. Record the chosen extractor and the reason as an issue, and flag every cell where the two disagree, with both values shown. Disagreement is itself a warning.
  - If both fail the checks, return the text output with status `failed` and the vision output attached for review.
  
  ➡️ Yes. The better check result wins, text wins ties, and there is no cell-level merging. Both outputs are kept and every disagreement is flagged. When both fail, the text output is returned as `failed` with the vision output attached.

⏺ I recorded this as D8. Every percentage in the report carries raw counts next to it. The plain-LLM baseline is marked "if time allows", and the report will state how many runs it took, since LLM output varies between runs.
  
  That leaves the open point from D4, the tolerance edge.
  
  ❓ **Q11 - Exact tolerance formula**: Your rule is 0.5 per item. The printed subtotal is itself rounded, so its error adds up to another 0.5. The strict bound is 0.5 × (items + 1). Printed numbers are whole, so the allowed difference is that bound rounded down.
  
  | Items | Your rule | Strict |
  |---|---|---|
  | 2 | 1 | 1 |
  | 3 | 1 | 2 |
  | 10 | 5 | 5 |
  | 11 | 5 | 6 |
  
  The two rules differ only for odd item counts. Under your rule, a correct extraction could be marked as a failure, which is costly since failures aren't just warnings. The 流動資産 differences on this page (3 and 2, with 10 items each) pass under both. The rule would apply to every sum check: section subtotals, gross plus contra equals net, and current plus fixed equals total.
  
  ➡️ Use the strict bound, `floor(0.5 × (items + 1))`, so a correctly extracted page can't fail on rounding alone. Anything above it is a failure. Every difference is reported either way, with the allowed bound shown beside it.

║ covered.

⏺ Bash(touch src/bsparser/__init__.py && cat > src/bsparser/amounts.py <<'EOF'
  from dataclasses import dataclass
  from typing import Optional
  
  
  @dataclass(frozen=True)
  class Amount:
      value: Optional[int]
  
  
  def parse_amount(raw: str) -> Amount:
      return Amount(value=int(raw.replace(",", "")))

⏺ Bash(cat > src/bsparser/amounts.py <<'EOF'
  from dataclasses import dataclass
  from typing import Optional
  
  
  @dataclass(frozen=True)
  class Amount:
      value: Optional[int]
      kind: str
  
  
  def parse_amount(raw: str) -> Amount:
      text = raw.strip()
      if text == "-":
          return Amount(value=None, kind="nil")
      negative = text.startswith("(") and text.endswith(")")
      if negative:
          text = text[1:-1]
      value = int(text.replace(",", ""))
      return Amount(value=-value if negative else value, kind="value")
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short 2>&1 | tail -3 && git add -A && git commit -q -m "$(cat <<'EOF'
  feat: parenthesised amounts parse as negative
  
  Co-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
  EOF
  )" && git log --oneline | head -1)

⏺ Bash(SP=/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-teja-Desktop-ur-ai-parser-setup/a3e0201a-6f55-434c-9bf7-ac47dda76ff4/scratchpad; mkdir -p $SP && cat > $SP/build_fixture_json.py <<'EOF'
  import json, re, subprocess, sys
  root = "/Users/teja/Desktop/ur-ai-parser-setup"
  text = subprocess.run(["pdftotext", "-layout", f"{root}/data/balance_sheet_after_correction.pdf", "-"],
                        capture_output=True, text=True).stdout
  num = r"[△－\-\d,]+"
  page_rows = []
  for line in text.splitlines():
      m = re.match(rf"^\s*(\S.*?)\s{{2,}}({num})\s+({num})\s*$", line)
      if m and not line.strip().startswith(("前連結", "(2025")):
          page_rows.append((m.group(1), m.group(2), m.group(3)))
      elif line.strip() in ("資産の部", "流動資産", "固定資産", "有形固定資産", "無形固定資産", "投資その他の資産"):
          page_rows.append((line.strip(), None, None))
  tmpl = []
  for line in open(f"{root}/data/gold/balance_sheet_after_correction.template.md", encoding="utf-8"):
      if re.match(r"^\| \d+ \|", line):
          c = [x.strip() for x in line.strip().strip("|").split("|")]
          tmpl.append(dict(n=int(c[0]), path=c[1], ja=c[2], en=c[3], role=c[4]))
  print(len(page_rows), len(tmpl), file=sys.stderr)
  assert len(page_rows) == len(tmpl) == 43
  dashes = set()
  def conv(raw):
      if raw is None: return None
      dashes.update(ch for ch in raw if not ch.isdigit() and ch != ",")
      if raw in ("－", "-", "−", "—", "―"): return {"raw": "-", "value": None}
      neg = raw.startswith("△")
      digits = raw.lstrip("△").replace(",", "")
      v = int(digits)
      return {"raw": f"({int(digits):,})" if neg else f"{v:,}", "value": -v if neg else v}
  rows = []
  for (lab, a, b), t in zip(page_rows, tmpl):
      norm = lambda s: re.sub(r"[\s()（）]", "", s)
      assert norm(lab) == norm(t["ja"]), (lab, t["ja"])
      depth = 0 if t["path"] == "" else t["path"].count("/") + 1
      rows.append(dict(depth=depth, role=t["role"], label=t["en"],
                       values=None if a is None else [conv(a), conv(b)]))
  print("non-digit chars seen in numbers:", [hex(ord(c)) for c in dashes], file=sys.stderr)
  json.dump(dict(unit="thousand_yen", unit_raw="in thousands of yen",
                 periods=["2025-03-31", "2026-03-31"], rows=rows),

  def section_difference(rows, header, total, period_index):
      """Printed subtotal minus the sum of the section's items (nil counted as 0 here only)."""
      start = next(i for i, r in enumerate(rows) if r["label"] == header)
      end = next(i for i, r in enumerate(rows) if r["label"] == total)
      items = [r for r in rows[start + 1:end] if r["role"] in ("item", "contra")]
      summed = sum((r["values"][period_index] or {"value": 0})["value"] or 0 for r in items)
      return rows[end]["values"][period_index]["value"] - summed
  
  
  def test_fixture_keeps_the_pages_real_rounding_on_current_assets():
      rows = load_fixture()["rows"]
      assert section_difference(rows, "Current assets", "Total current assets", 0) == 3
      assert section_difference(rows, "Current assets", "Total current assets", 1) == 2
  EOF

  """Render data/fixtures/english_balance_sheet.json to a one-page PDF.
  
  Run by hand: .venv/bin/python tests/make_english_fixture.py
  The generated PDF is committed; tests do not regenerate it.
  """
  import json
  from pathlib import Path
  
  from reportlab.lib.pagesizes import A4
  from reportlab.pdfgen import canvas
  
  DATA = Path(__file__).parent.parent / "data" / "fixtures" / "english_balance_sheet.json"
  LEFT, INDENT, COL1, COL2, TOP, STEP = 60, 12, 420, 520, 740, 14.5
  
  
  def render(data, out):
      c = canvas.Canvas(str(out), pagesize=A4)
      c.setFont("Helvetica", 9)
      c.drawString(LEFT, 790, "3. Consolidated financial statements and notes")
      c.drawString(LEFT + 8, 776, "(1) Consolidated balance sheet")
      c.drawRightString(COL2, 762, f"({data['unit_raw']})")
      c.drawRightString(COL1, TOP, "Prior fiscal year")
      c.drawRightString(COL2, TOP, "Current fiscal year")
      c.drawRightString(COL1, TOP - 11, "(Mar. 31, 2025)")
      c.drawRightString(COL2, TOP - 11, "(Mar. 31, 2026)")
      y = TOP - 30
      for row in data["rows"]:
          c.drawString(LEFT + INDENT * row["depth"], y, row["label"])
          if row["values"]:
              c.drawRightString(COL1, y, row["values"][0]["raw"])
              c.drawRightString(COL2, y, row["values"][1]["raw"])
          y -= STEP
      c.showPage()
      c.save()
  
  
  if __name__ == "__main__":
      render(json.loads(DATA.read_text(encoding="utf-8")), DATA.with_suffix(".pdf"))
  EOF

⏺ The English fixture is done and committed (12 tests pass):

  - **Generator:** `tests/make_english_fixture.py` is run by hand. `reportlab` is added to `requirements.txt`.
  - **Numbers:** they come straight from the real PDF's text, and the labels match your template row by row. The page's real rounding differences are kept: 1 to 3 on 13 of the 16 checks, 0 on the other 3. The page's own 流動資産 differences (3 and 2) are pinned in a test.
  
  Two of my steps were not red-first. The data-file and rounding-pin tests passed on first run, so they are guards. The PDF test was red, as it should be.
  
  Next is English rows, sections and roles from the fixture PDF with pdfplumber. The first test is that the parser returns 43 rows with the right depth for each.
  
  Before that slice, one design choice is yours.
  
  ❓ **Q15 - How depth and sections are read from the page**: On the real page and the fixture, the only signal for nesting is the label's left x position, since there are no lines or numbering. I suggest the parser groups the distinct left positions into levels (smallest = 0) instead of using fixed pixel steps, and flags an issue if a row's x doesn't fit any level. Section path and role then come from that depth, from the neighbours, and from label patterns. `（純額）` and `, net` mark net rows. 減価償却累計額 and "Accumulated depreciation" mark contra rows. A row with values and a label starting with 合計 or "Total" is a subtotal. A header is a row with no values. Role rules are written one test at a time.
  
  ➡️ Yes. Levels come from clustering the distinct x positions and are not hard-coded. An x that fits no level is flagged. Roles come from depth, neighbours and label patterns, written test by test.

║ with it. English "Total ..." starts with it. Test both. Second, the grand

║ label with no values isn't always a header: a long label wrapped onto

⏺ Bash(cat > tests/test_pdf_lines.py <<'EOF'
  from pathlib import Path
  
  from bsparser.pdf_lines import extract_lines
  
  FIXTURE_PDF = Path(__file__).parent.parent / "data" / "fixtures" / "english_balance_sheet.pdf"
  
  
  def find(lines, label):
      matches = [line for line in lines if line.label == label]
      assert len(matches) == 1, (label, len(matches))
      return matches[0]
  
  
  def test_line_has_label_left_x_and_raw_amounts():
      lines = extract_lines(FIXTURE_PDF)
      cash = find(lines, "Cash and deposits")
      assert cash.amounts == ["2,002,429", "608,281"]
      assert cash.x0 > 0
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short tests/test_pdf_lines.py 2>&1 | tail -4
  cat > src/bsparser/pdf_lines.py <<'EOF'
  from dataclasses import dataclass, field
  from typing import List
  
  import pdfplumber
  
  from bsparser.amounts import parse_amount
  
  LINE_TOLERANCE = 2.0  # points: words whose tops differ by less sit on one line
  
  
  @dataclass
  class Line:
      label: str
      x0: float
      top: float
      amounts: List[str] = field(default_factory=list)
  
  
  def _is_amount(word: str) -> bool:
      return parse_amount(word).kind in ("value", "nil")
  
  
  def extract_lines(pdf_path) -> List[Line]:
      with pdfplumber.open(pdf_path) as pdf:
          words = pdf.pages[0].extract_words()
      rows: List[List[dict]] = []
      for word in sorted(words, key=lambda w: (w["top"], w["x0"])):
          if rows and abs(rows[-1][0]["top"] - word["top"]) <= LINE_TOLERANCE:
              rows[-1].append(word)
          else:
              rows.append([word])
      lines = []
      for row in rows:
          row.sort(key=lambda w: w["x0"])
          split = len(row)
          while split > 0 and _is_amount(row[split - 1]["text"]):
              split -= 1
          lines.append(Line(
              label=" ".join(w["text"] for w in row[:split]),
              x0=row[0]["x0"],
              top=row[0]["top"],
              amounts=[w["text"] for w in row[split:]],
          ))
      return lines
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short 2>&1 | tail -3)

  from bsparser.levels import assign_levels
  
  
  def test_x_positions_within_tolerance_share_a_level():
      xs = [60.0, 60.4, 72.1, 71.8, 84.0, 59.7]
      assert assign_levels(xs, tolerance=2.0) == [0, 0, 1, 1, 2, 0]
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short tests/test_levels.py 2>&1 | tail -3
  cat > src/bsparser/levels.py <<'EOF'
  from typing import List
  
  
  def assign_levels(xs: List[float], tolerance: float) -> List[int]:
      """Level 0 is the leftmost cluster. Positions within `tolerance` of the previous one share a cluster."""
      clusters: List[float] = []  # last x seen in each cluster, left to right
      for x in sorted(xs):
          if clusters and x - clusters[-1] <= tolerance:
              clusters[-1] = x
          else:
              clusters.append(x)
      return [next(i for i, last in enumerate(clusters) if x <= last + 1e-9) for x in xs]
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short 2>&1 | tail -3)

⏺ Test 5 is your third fix. A no-value line followed by a line at the same indent that has values looks like a wrapped label. The parser must flag it and must not treat it as a header.

⏺ Bash(cat > tests/test_wrapped.py <<'EOF'
  from bsparser.wrapped import LineInfo, find_possible_wrapped
  
  
  def test_novalue_line_followed_by_same_level_line_with_values_is_flagged():
      lines = [
          LineInfo("Current assets", False, 1, 100.0),
          LineInfo("Allowance for doubtful accounts and other", False, 2, 114.0),
          LineInfo("long-term receivables", True, 2, 128.0),
      ]
      flagged = find_possible_wrapped(lines)
      assert [f.index for f in flagged] == [1]
      assert flagged[0].reason == "next line at the same level has values"
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short tests/test_wrapped.py 2>&1 | tail -3
  cat > src/bsparser/wrapped.py <<'EOF'
  from dataclasses import dataclass
  from typing import List
  
  
  @dataclass
  class LineInfo:
      label: str
      has_values: bool
      level: int
      top: float
  
  
  @dataclass
  class WrappedFlag:
      index: int
      reason: str
  
  
  def find_possible_wrapped(lines: List[LineInfo]) -> List[WrappedFlag]:
      flagged = []
      for i, line in enumerate(lines[:-1]):
          nxt = lines[i + 1]
          if not line.has_values and nxt.has_values and nxt.level == line.level:
              flagged.append(WrappedFlag(i, "next line at the same level has values"))
      return flagged
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short 2>&1 | tail -2; git add -A; git commit -q -m "$(cat <<'EOF'
  feat: flag a no-value line followed by a same-level line with values as a possible wrapped label
  
  Co-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
  EOF
  )")

          LineInfo("Notes receivable", True, 2, 114.0),
          LineInfo("Investments in unconsolidated subsidiaries", False, 2, 128.0),
          LineInfo("and affiliates", True, 3, 136.0),
          LineInfo("Land", True, 2, 150.0),
          LineInfo("Buildings", True, 2, 164.0),
      ]
      flagged = find_possible_wrapped(lines)
      assert [f.index for f in flagged] == [2]
      assert flagged[0].reason == "next line is much closer than the usual line spacing"
  
  
  def test_header_with_normal_spacing_and_a_deeper_next_line_is_not_flagged():
      lines = [
          LineInfo("Current assets", False, 1, 100.0),
          LineInfo("Cash and deposits", True, 2, 114.0),
          LineInfo("Notes receivable", True, 2, 128.0),
      ]
      assert find_possible_wrapped(lines) == []
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short tests/test_wrapped.py 2>&1 | tail -4
  cat > src/bsparser/wrapped.py <<'EOF'
  from dataclasses import dataclass
  from statistics import median
  from typing import List
  
  TIGHT_RATIO = 0.7  # a gap below this share of the median spacing looks like a wrapped line
  
  
  @dataclass
  class LineInfo:
      label: str
      has_values: bool
      level: int
      top: float
  
  
  @dataclass
  class WrappedFlag:
      index: int
      reason: str
  
  
  def find_possible_wrapped(lines: List[LineInfo]) -> List[WrappedFlag]:
      gaps = [b.top - a.top for a, b in zip(lines, lines[1:])]
      usual = median(gaps) if gaps else 0.0
      flagged = []
      for i, line in enumerate(lines[:-1]):
          nxt = lines[i + 1]
          if line.has_values:
              continue
          if nxt.has_values and nxt.level == line.level:
              flagged.append(WrappedFlag(i, "next line at the same level has values"))
          elif gaps[i] < TIGHT_RATIO * usual:
              flagged.append(WrappedFlag(i, "next line is much closer than the usual line spacing"))
      return flagged
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short 2>&1 | tail -3)

     from bsparser.labels import is_net_label
     
     GRAND_TOTAL_LEVEL = 1  # one level under the top section header (資産の部 / Assets)
     
     
     def _is_total_label(label: str) -> bool:
         return label.startswith("Total") or label.endswith("合計")  # 合計 ends the label
     
     
     def classify_role(label: str, has_values: bool, level: int) -> str:
         if not has_values:
             return "header"  # a wrapped label can look like this; see wrapped.py
         if _is_total_label(label):
             return "total" if level == GRAND_TOTAL_LEVEL else "subtotal"
         if is_net_label(label) or label.endswith(", net"):
             return "net"
         if label.startswith("Accumulated depreciation") or label.startswith("\u6e1b\u4fa1\u511f\u5374\u7d2f\u8a08\u984d"):
             return "contra"  # 減価償却累計額
         return "item"

  import re
  from dataclasses import dataclass, field
  from typing import List
  
  from bsparser.pdf_lines import Line, extract_lines
  
  YEAR = re.compile(r"(?:19|20)\d{2}")
  
  
  @dataclass
  class Row:
      label: str
  
  
  @dataclass
  class Page:
      rows: List[Row] = field(default_factory=list)
  
  
  def _table_lines(lines: List[Line]) -> List[Line]:
      """Rows start below the last header line that carries two or more years."""
      start = 0
      for i, line in enumerate(lines):
          if len(YEAR.findall(line.label)) >= 2:
              start = i + 1
      return lines[start:]
  
  
  def parse_page(pdf_path) -> Page:
      return Page(rows=[Row(label=line.label) for line in _table_lines(extract_lines(pdf_path))])
  EOF

  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short tests/test_parse_page.py 2>&1 | tail -3
  cat > src/bsparser/page.py <<'EOF'
  import re
  from dataclasses import dataclass, field
  from typing import List
  
  from bsparser.levels import assign_levels
  from bsparser.pdf_lines import Line, extract_lines
  from bsparser.roles import classify_role
  
  YEAR = re.compile(r"(?:19|20)\d{2}")
  LEVEL_TOLERANCE = 2.0  # points
  
  
  @dataclass
  class Row:
      label: str
      level: int
      role: str
      path: List[str]
  
  
  @dataclass
  class Page:
      rows: List[Row] = field(default_factory=list)
  
  
  def _table_lines(lines: List[Line]) -> List[Line]:
      """Rows start below the last header line that carries two or more years."""
      start = 0
      for i, line in enumerate(lines):
          if len(YEAR.findall(line.label)) >= 2:
              start = i + 1
      return lines[start:]
  
  
  def build_rows(lines: List[Line]) -> List[Row]:
      levels = assign_levels([line.x0 for line in lines], LEVEL_TOLERANCE)
      rows: List[Row] = []
      stack: List[Row] = []  # nearest preceding row at each shallower level
      for line, level in zip(lines, levels):
          while stack and stack[-1].level >= level:
              stack.pop()
          role = classify_role(line.label, has_values=bool(line.amounts), level=level)
          row = Row(label=line.label, level=level, role=role, path=[r.label for r in stack])
          rows.append(row)
          stack.append(row)
      return rows
  
  
  def parse_page(pdf_path) -> Page:
      return Page(rows=build_rows(_table_lines(extract_lines(pdf_path))))
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short 2>&1 | tail -3)

⏺ `parse_page` now returns structured rows from the English fixture PDF. 28 tests pass, and every step is committed.
  
  - **What it returns:** 43 rows, each with an ordinal `id` (`r1` to `r43`), `label`, `level`, `role`, a `path` that runs through the gross item, and `parent`. A contra row and a net row both point to the gross row above. Every other row has no parent.

  A decision for you. Your template marks row 12 (貸倒引当金, allowance for doubtful accounts) as `contra`. The parser calls it `item`. It has no gross row to attach to, it sits inside the current-assets sum and its value is just negative, so it can't get a `parent` under D2.
  
  ❓ **Q16 - Role of the allowance row**: I suggest `contra` stays only for lines that link to a gross row (the 減価償却累計額 lines). The allowance is an `item` with a negative value.   ➡️ Yes. The allowance row is an `item` with a negative value, and I'll update the template and note it as D12.

  cat > src/bsparser/periods.py <<'EOF'
  import re
  from typing import List
  
  MONTHS = {m: i for i, m in enumerate(
      ["jan", "feb", "mar", "apr", "may", "jun", "jul", "aug", "sep", "oct", "nov", "dec"], start=1)}
  ENGLISH_DATE = re.compile(r"\b([A-Za-z]{3})[a-z]*\.? (\d{1,2}), (\d{4})\b")
  
  
  def find_periods(text: str) -> List[str]:
      """ISO dates in the order they are printed, e.g. 'Mar. 31, 2025' -> '2025-03-31'."""
      found = []
      for month, day, year in ENGLISH_DATE.findall(text):
          number = MONTHS.get(month.lower())
          if number:
              found.append(f"{int(year):04d}-{number:02d}-{int(day):02d}")
      return found
  EOF
  python3 - <<'EOF'
  p = "/Users/teja/Desktop/ur-ai-parser-setup/src/bsparser/page.py"

  s = s.replace("    issues: List[Issue] = field(default_factory=list)\n\n\nNET", "    issues: List[Issue] = field(default_factory=list)\n    periods: List[str] = field(default_factory=list)\n\n\nNET")
  s = s.replace('''def _table_lines(lines: List[Line]) -> List[Line]:
      """Rows start below the last header line that carries two or more years."""
      start = 0
      for i, line in enumerate(lines):
          if len(YEAR.findall(line.label)) >= 2:
              start = i + 1
      return lines[start:]
  ''', '''def _split_header(lines: List[Line]):
      """Rows start below the last header line that carries two or more years."""
      start = 0
      for i, line in enumerate(lines):
          if len(YEAR.findall(line.label)) >= 2:
              start = i + 1
      return lines[:start], lines[start:]
  ''')
  s = s.replace('''def parse_page(pdf_path) -> Page:
      return build_page(_table_lines(extract_lines(pdf_path)))''', '''def parse_page(pdf_path) -> Page:
      header, table = _split_header(extract_lines(pdf_path))
      page = build_page(table)
      page.periods = find_periods(" ".join(line.label for line in header))
      return page''')
  open(p, "w", encoding="utf-8").write(s)
  EOF

  cat > src/bsparser/units.py <<'EOF'
  import re
  from typing import Optional, Tuple
  
  ENGLISH_UNITS = [
      (re.compile(r"in thousands of yen", re.I), "thousand_yen"),
      (re.compile(r"in millions of yen", re.I), "million_yen"),
  ]
  
  
  def find_unit(text: str) -> Optional[Tuple[str, str]]:
      """Return (canonical unit, text as printed), or None when no known unit is printed."""
      for pattern, unit in ENGLISH_UNITS:
          match = pattern.search(text)
          if match:
              return unit, match.group(0)
      return None
  EOF
  python3 - <<'EOF'

     def parse_lines(lines: List[Line]) -> Page:
         header, table = _split_header(lines)
         page = build_page(table)
         header_text = " ".join(line.label for line in header)
         page.periods = find_periods(header_text)
         found = find_unit(header_text)
         if found:
             page.unit, page.unit_raw = found
         else:
             page.issues.append(Issue("unit_missing", "", "no known unit found in the header; none assumed"))
         for row in page.rows:
             if row.values and len(row.values) != len(page.periods):
                 page.issues.append(Issue(
                     "column_count_mismatch", row.id,
                     f"{len(row.values)} values but {len(page.periods)} period columns; none assigned by guess"))
         return page

  from pathlib import Path
  
  from bsparser.page import parse_page
  from bsparser.records import make_records
  
  FIXTURE_PDF = Path(__file__).parent.parent / "data" / "fixtures" / "english_balance_sheet.pdf"
  
  
  def test_one_record_per_value_row_per_period_with_the_d1_fields():
      records = make_records(parse_page(FIXTURE_PDF))
      cash = [r for r in records if r["label"] == "Cash and deposits" and r["period"] == "2026-03-31"]
      assert cash == [{
          "id": "r3|2026-03-31",
          "path": ["Assets", "Current assets"],
          "label": "Cash and deposits",
          "label_en": "Cash and deposits",
          "label_en_source": "original",
          "role": "item",
          "parent": None,
          "period": "2026-03-31",
          "value": 608281,
          "kind": "value",
          "raw": "608,281",

          "warnings": [],
      }]

  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short tests/test_records.py 2>&1 | tail -3
  cat > src/bsparser/records.py <<'EOF'
  from typing import Dict, List
  
  from bsparser.page import Page
  
  
  def make_records(page: Page) -> List[Dict]:
      """One record per value row per period (D1). Header rows have no value and stay in page.rows."""
      records = []
      for row in page.rows:
          if not row.values or len(row.values) != len(page.periods):
              continue  # a column-count mismatch is already an issue; nothing is assigned by guess
          for period, amount in zip(page.periods, row.values):
              records.append({
                  "id": f"{row.id}|{period}",
                  "path": list(row.path),
                  "label": row.label,
                  "label_en": row.label,
                  "label_en_source": "original",
                  "role": row.role,
                  "parent": f"{row.parent}|{period}" if row.parent else None,
                  "period": period,
                  "value": amount.value,
                  "kind": amount.kind,
                  "raw": amount.raw,
                  "unit": page.unit,
                  "warnings": [],
              })
      return records
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short 2>&1 | tail -3)

⏺ Bash(cat > tests/test_checks.py <<'EOF'
  from pathlib import Path
  
  from bsparser.checks import run_checks
  from bsparser.page import parse_page
  
  FIXTURE_PDF = Path(__file__).parent.parent / "data" / "fixtures" / "english_balance_sheet.pdf"
  
  
  def check_for(checks, label, period):
      found = [c for c in checks if c.label == label and c.period == period]
      assert len(found) == 1, (label, period, len(found))
      return found[0]
  
  
  def test_net_equals_gross_plus_contra_reports_difference_and_bound():
      checks = run_checks(parse_page(FIXTURE_PDF))
      c = check_for(checks, "Buildings and structures, net", "2026-03-31")
      assert (c.status, c.difference, c.bound, c.items) == ("pass", -1, 1, 2)
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short tests/test_checks.py 2>&1 | tail -3
  cat > src/bsparser/checks.py <<'EOF'
  from dataclasses import dataclass
  from typing import List
  
  from bsparser.page import Page
  
  
  @dataclass
  class Check:
      label: str  # the printed total the check judges
      row_id: str
      period: str
      expected: int  # sum of the contributing items
      printed: int
      difference: int  # printed minus expected
      bound: int
      items: int  # contributors that carry a rounded number
      status: str  # pass | failed | skipped
      reason: str = ""
  
  
  def allowed_bound(items: int) -> int:
      """Strict bound (D4): each item and the printed total are rounded, so 0.5 x (items + 1), floored."""
      return (items + 1) // 2
  
  
  def run_checks(page: Page) -> List[Check]:
      by_id = {r.id: r for r in page.rows}
      checks = []
      for row in page.rows:
          if row.role != "net" or row.parent is None:
              continue
          contra = [r for r in page.rows if r.role == "contra" and r.parent == row.parent]
          gross = by_id[row.parent]
          for p, period in enumerate(page.periods):
              expected = gross.values[p].value + sum(c.values[p].value for c in contra)
              printed = row.values[p].value
              checks.append(Check(row.label, row.id, period, expected, printed, printed - expected,
                                  allowed_bound(2), 2, "pass"))
      return checks
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short 2>&1 | tail -3)

      over = check_for(checks, "Total current assets", "2026-03-31")

⏺ The long per-period records and the identity checks with the strict bound are done and committed. 43 tests pass.
  
  **Records** (`src/bsparser/records.py`):
  - **Shape:** one record per value row per period, with the D1 fields plus `kind`.
  - **ids:** `id` looks like `r3|2026-03-31`. A contra or net record's `parent` points to the gross record of the same period.
  - **Nil:** it keeps its raw text, so `-` stays `-`.
  
  **Identity checks** (`src/bsparser/checks.py`):
  - **Coverage:** `net = gross + contra`, section subtotals and totals of subtotals (non-current assets, total assets). That gives 20 checks on the English fixture, and all pass.
  - **Bound:** `floor(0.5 × (items + 1))`, with the difference and the bound shown on every check.
  - **Edges tested:** a difference equal to the bound passes and one more fails. The real prior-year non-current assets total (3 subtotals, off by 2) passes under the strict bound, where the naive rule would have failed it. The page's 3-off property subtotal sits exactly on its bound. Net lines use the net value when summing a section.
  - **Skipped:** a check that involves an unreadable value comes out as `skipped`, with the row ids in the reason and no numbers.
  
  Things I decided or got wrong:
  - **My calls:**
    - Header rows get no per-period record. They stay in `rows`, since they have no value to put in one.
    - On an English page, `label_en` equals the label and `label_en_source` is `original`. Japanese rows will say `translation`.
    - Nil items aren't counted toward the bound, because a nil has no rounding error. D4 doesn't say this explicitly, so tell me if you want nil counted.
  - **TDD slips:** the property-subtotal test passed without a red step because I had already written the net substitution. The fixture-wide test is also a guard.
  - **Known gap:** an unreadable number never reaches the checks from a real PDF yet. A garbled token like `1,2x4` is read as part of the label, so the row ends up with too few values and a `column_count_mismatch` issue. That is visible and not silent, but D6's `unreadable` value kind only arises in tests for now. I'd like a test for that.
  
  The next slice is the page status (worst of unreadable, failed, ok) and the JSON output, then the Japanese header, units and Reiwa dates, then the real PDF. Your gold values are still needed from step 4 onwards, so tell me when they're ready.

  from pathlib import Path
  
  from bsparser.amounts import Amount
  from bsparser.checks import run_checks
  from bsparser.page import parse_lines, parse_page
  from bsparser.pdf_lines import Line
  from bsparser.status import page_status
  
  FIXTURE_PDF = Path(__file__).parent.parent / "data" / "fixtures" / "english_balance_sheet.pdf"
  
  
  def small_page(total_prior="602", total_current="603", other=("300", "300")):
      return parse_lines([
          Line("(in thousands of yen)", 400.0, 70.0),
          Line("(Mar. 31, 2025) (Mar. 31, 2026)", 300.0, 94.0),

          Line("Total current assets", 84.0, 190.0, [total_prior, total_current]),
      ])

  cat > src/bsparser/status.py <<'EOF'
  from typing import List
  
  from bsparser.checks import Check
  from bsparser.page import Page
  
  # D6/D9: the page status is the worst issue. unreadable over failed over ok.
  UNREADABLE_ISSUES = {"unreadable_value", "unit_missing", "column_count_mismatch"}
  FAILED_ISSUES = {"net_label_mismatch"}
  
  
  def page_status(page: Page, checks: List[Check]) -> str:
      if any(i.kind in UNREADABLE_ISSUES for i in page.issues):
          return "unreadable"
      if any(c.status == "failed" for c in checks) or any(i.kind in FAILED_ISSUES for i in page.issues):
          return "failed"
      return "ok"
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short 2>&1 | tail -3)

  def test_failed_check_makes_the_page_failed():

      assert statuses == ["failed", "skipped"]  # the two states exist side by side

  import json
  from pathlib import Path
  
  from bsparser.output import build_output
  
  FIXTURE_PDF = Path(__file__).parent.parent / "data" / "fixtures" / "english_balance_sheet.pdf"
  
  
  def test_output_has_status_unit_periods_rows_records_checks_and_issues_and_serialises():
      out = build_output(FIXTURE_PDF)
      assert out["status"] == "ok"
      assert (out["unit"], out["unit_raw"]) == ("thousand_yen", "in thousands of yen")
      assert out["periods"] == ["2025-03-31", "2026-03-31"]

  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short tests/test_output.py 2>&1 | tail -3
  cat > src/bsparser/output.py <<'EOF'
  from dataclasses import asdict
  from typing import Dict
  
  from bsparser.checks import run_checks
  from bsparser.page import parse_page
  from bsparser.records import make_records
  from bsparser.status import page_status
  
  
  def build_output(pdf_path) -> Dict:
      page = parse_page(pdf_path)
      checks = run_checks(page)
      return {
          "source": str(pdf_path),
          "status": page_status(page, checks),
          "unit": page.unit,
          "unit_raw": page.unit_raw,
          "periods": page.periods,
          "issues": [asdict(i) for i in page.issues],
          "checks": [asdict(c) for c in checks],
          "rows": [
              {"id": r.id, "label": r.label, "label_en": r.label, "label_en_source": "original",
               "role": r.role, "level": r.level, "parent": r.parent, "path": list(r.path)}
              for r in page.rows
          ],
          "records": make_records(page),
      }
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short 2>&1 | tail -3)

  def test_write_outputs_writes_the_json_source_of_truth_and_a_csv_view_of_the_records(tmp_path):
      import csv
  
      from bsparser.output import write_outputs
  
      json_path, csv_path = write_outputs(FIXTURE_PDF, tmp_path)

  cat >> src/bsparser/output.py <<'EOF'
  
  
  RECORD_COLUMNS = ["id", "path", "label", "label_en", "label_en_source", "role", "parent",
                    "period", "value", "kind", "raw", "unit", "warnings"]
  
  
  def write_outputs(pdf_path, out_dir):
      """JSON is the source of truth (D1); the records CSV is a view of it."""
      out_dir = Path(out_dir)
      out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
      out = build_output(pdf_path)
      stem = Path(pdf_path).stem
      json_path = out_dir / f"{stem}.json"
      json_path.write_text(json.dumps(out, ensure_ascii=False, indent=1), encoding="utf-8")
      csv_path = out_dir / f"{stem}.records.csv"
      with csv_path.open("w", encoding="utf-8", newline="") as f:
          writer = csv.DictWriter(f, fieldnames=RECORD_COLUMNS)
          writer.writeheader()
          for rec in out["records"]:
              row = dict(rec)
              row["path"] = " / ".join(rec["path"])
              row["value"] = "" if rec["value"] is None else rec["value"]
              row["parent"] = rec["parent"] or ""
              row["warnings"] = "; ".join(rec["warnings"])
              writer.writerow(row)
      return json_path, csv_path
  EOF
  python3 - <<'EOF'

  open(p, "w", encoding="utf-8").write(s)
  EOF

  import json
  import sys
  
  from bsparser.output import write_outputs
  
  
  def main(argv) -> int:
      if len(argv) != 2:
          print("usage: python -m bsparser <pdf> <out_dir>", file=sys.stderr)
          return 2
      json_path, csv_path = write_outputs(argv[0], argv[1])
      out = json.loads(json_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
      print(f"status: {out['status']}  issues: {len(out['issues'])}  "
            f"checks: {len(out['checks'])}  records: {len(out['records'])}")
      print(f"wrote {json_path} and {csv_path}")
      return 0
  
  
  if __name__ == "__main__":
      sys.exit(main(sys.argv[1:]))
  EOF

  - **D4:** it now says nil items don't count toward the bound, and each check reports how many items it counted.
  - **Garbled token:** a token that is mostly digits and commas with one stray letter (like `1,2x4`) is now an amount token, not label text. It becomes an `unreadable` value with its raw text kept. It gets a page issue (`unreadable_value`, naming the row) and a record warning. Year tokens like `2025)` and words like `Q3` are not mistaken for amounts, and a test covers that.
  - **Page status:** it is the worst issue. `unreadable_value`, `unit_missing` and `column_count_mismatch` give `unreadable`. A failed check or a `net_label_mismatch` gives `failed`. Otherwise it is `ok`. A page with both an unreadable value and a failed check reports `unreadable`, and both issues stay listed. I picked the issue-to-severity mapping myself. Tell me if you'd rank any of them differently.
  - **JSON output:** it holds `status`, `unit`, `unit_raw`, `periods`, `issues`, `checks`, `rows` and `records`. A records CSV is written alongside it. Nil stays empty in the CSV and never becomes 0. `python -m bsparser <pdf> <out_dir>` runs it. On the English fixture it gives status `ok`, 0 issues, 20 checks and 74 records.

  def header_lines(date_line):
      from bsparser.pdf_lines import Line
  
      return [
          Line("（単位：千円）", 400.0, 70.0),
          Line(date_line, 300.0, 94.0),
          Line("資産の部", 60.0, 120.0),
          Line("流動資産", 72.0, 134.0),

      ]

      assert page.periods == ["2025-03-31", "2026-03-31"]
      assert [r.label for r in page.rows] == ["資産の部", "流動資産", "現金及び預金"]
      assert page.unit == "thousand_yen"

  from pathlib import Path

  cat >> src/bsparser/labels.py <<'EOF'
  
  
  import json
  from pathlib import Path
  from typing import Optional, Tuple
  
  DICTIONARY_PATH = Path(__file__).parent.parent.parent / "data" / "labels_ja_en.json"
  
  
  def _load_dictionary():
      return json.loads(DICTIONARY_PATH.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
  
  
  def english_label(label: str) -> Tuple[Optional[str], str]:
      """(label_en, source). For reading only: never used in matching or in any check."""
      dictionary = _load_dictionary()
      translated = dictionary["entries"].get(label)
      if translated is None:
          return None, "missing"
      status = "reviewed" if dictionary["review_status"] == "reviewed_by_reviewer_session" else "unreviewed"
      return translated, f"translation_{status}"
  EOF

⏺ Bash(cat > tests/test_gold.py <<'EOF'
  from bsparser.gold import load_gold
  
  TEMPLATE = """# Gold template
  | # | path | label (as printed) | label_en (translation) | role | why tricky | raw 2025-03-31 | raw 2026-03-31 |
  |---|------|--------------------|------------------------|------|------------|----------------|----------------|
  | 1 | | 資産の部 | Assets | header | | | |
  | 3 | 資産の部/流動資産 | 現金及び預金 | Cash and deposits | item | | 2,002,429 | 608,281 |
  | 12 | 資産の部/流動資産 | 貸倒引当金 | Allowance for doubtful accounts | item | negative mark | △2,979 | △0 |
  | 26 | 資産の部/固定資産/有形固定資産 | 建設仮勘定 | Construction in progress | item | printed dash | － | 4,553 |
  | 30 | 資産の部/固定資産/有形固定資産 | 有形固定資産合計 | Total property | subtotal | | | |
  """
  
  
  def test_only_filled_cells_become_gold_records_keyed_by_path_label_and_period(tmp_path):
      path = tmp_path / "template.md"
      path.write_text(TEMPLATE, encoding="utf-8")
      gold = load_gold(path)
      assert len(gold) == 6  # 3 rows x 2 periods; header and empty rows give none
      cash = [g for g in gold if g.label == "現金及び預金" and g.period == "2026-03-31"][0]
      assert (cash.path, cash.role, cash.raw) == (["資産の部", "流動資産"], "item", "608,281")
      zero = [g for g in gold if g.label == "貸倒引当金" and g.period == "2026-03-31"][0]
      assert zero.raw == "△0"
      nil = [g for g in gold if g.label == "建設仮勘定" and g.period == "2025-03-31"][0]
      assert nil.raw == "－"
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short tests/test_gold.py 2>&1 | tail -3
  cat > src/bsparser/gold.py <<'EOF'
  import re
  from dataclasses import dataclass
  from pathlib import Path
  from typing import List
  
  
  @dataclass
  class Gold:
      path: List[str]
      label: str
      role: str
      period: str
      raw: str  # as printed on the page, read by the user from the image
  
  
  PERIOD_COLUMN = re.compile(r"raw (\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2})")
  
  
  def _cells(line: str) -> List[str]:
      return [c.strip() for c in line.strip().strip("|").split("|")]
  
  
  def load_gold(template_path) -> List[Gold]:
      """Filled cells of the gold template. The key is (path, label, period), never the row number."""
      periods: List[str] = []
      gold: List[Gold] = []
      for line in Path(template_path).read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines():
          if line.startswith("| #"):
              periods = PERIOD_COLUMN.findall(line)
          elif re.match(r"^\| \d+ \|", line):
              cells = _cells(line)
              path = [p for p in cells[1].split("/") if p]
              for period, raw in zip(periods, cells[6:6 + len(periods)]):
                  if raw:
                      gold.append(Gold(path, cells[2], cells[4], period, raw))
      return gold
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short 2>&1 | tail -3)

  cat >> src/bsparser/gold.py <<'EOF'
  
  
  @dataclass
  class Result:
      gold: Gold
      status: str  # exact | wrong_value | wrong_role | not_found | ambiguous
      flagged: bool  # the parser warned about this record, its row, or the whole page
  
  
  def evaluate(output, golds: List[Gold]) -> List[Result]:
      results = []
      for g in golds:
          matches = [r for r in output["records"]
                     if r["path"] == g.path and r["label"] == g.label and r["period"] == g.period]
          if len(matches) != 1:
              results.append(Result(g, "not_found" if not matches else "ambiguous", True))
              continue
          rec = matches[0]
          flagged = bool(rec["warnings"]) or output["status"] != "ok"
          status = "exact" if rec["raw"] == g.raw else "wrong_value"
          results.append(Result(g, status, flagged))
      return results
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short 2>&1 | tail -3)

  s = open(p, encoding="utf-8").read()
  note = """## D15. How gold is compared (implemented, built before any gold values exist)
  - Gold is the filled cells of `data/gold/balance_sheet_after_correction.template.md`. The user reads the values from the image and types them as printed (raw text). Key: (path, label, period). Never the row number.
  - Layer 1 (value): the printed text must match. Typing noise is ignored (full-width versus ASCII digits, spaces, a plain `-` for `－`). Marks are not noise: `△0` is not `(0)`.
  - Layer 2 (structure): the role must match. The path is part of the key, so a wrong path shows as `not_found`.
  - A key that matches zero records is `not_found`, several is `ambiguous`. Both count as errors and are flagged. Never a guess.
  - A result is "flagged" when the record has a warning or the page status is not `ok`. Otherwise a wrong result is a silent error. The report counts the two apart (D4, D8).
  - Known limit: gold is compared as printed text, and the parser's own sign and nil logic is tested separately with unit tests. A wrong sign rule would not be caught by gold alone. State this in the report.
  - Gold values are read from the image by the user, never from the parser or the extracted text.
  
  """
  s = s.replace("## D14. English labels for Japanese pages", note + "## D14. English labels for Japanese pages", 1)
  open(p, "w", encoding="utf-8").write(s)
  EOF

  cat > src/bsparser/baseline.py <<'EOF'
  from typing import List
  
  from bsparser.gold import Gold, Result, _same_text
  from bsparser.page import _split_header
  from bsparser.pdf_lines import extract_lines
  from bsparser.periods import find_periods
  
  
  def first_match(pdf_path, golds: List[Gold]) -> List[Result]:
      """Naive baseline: the first line whose label matches, the section path ignored, the column by period order."""
      lines = extract_lines(pdf_path)
      header, table = _split_header(lines)
      periods = find_periods(" ".join(line.label for line in header))
      results = []
      for g in golds:
          line = next((line for line in table if line.label == g.label), None)
          if line is None or g.period not in periods or periods.index(g.period) >= len(line.amounts):
              results.append(Result(g, "not_found", False))
              continue
          ok = _same_text(line.amounts[periods.index(g.period)], g.raw)
          results.append(Result(g, "exact" if ok else "wrong_value", False, value_ok=ok))
      return results
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short 2>&1 | tail -3)

  from pathlib import Path
  
  from bsparser.baseline import first_match
  from bsparser.gold import Gold, evaluate
  from bsparser.output import build_output
  from bsparser.report import summarise
  
  REAL_PDF = Path(__file__).parent.parent / "data" / "balance_sheet_after_correction.pdf"
  CURRENT = ["資産の部", "流動資産"]
  INTANGIBLE = ["資産の部", "固定資産", "無形固定資産"]

      Gold(CURRENT, "現金及び預金", "item", "2026-03-31", "608,281"),
      Gold(CURRENT, "その他", "item", "2025-03-31", "105,260"),
      Gold(INTANGIBLE, "その他", "item", "2025-03-31", "10,070"),
  ]
  
  
  def test_every_slice_shows_raw_counts_next_to_the_percentage():

      table = summarise(out, GOLDS, evaluate(out, GOLDS), first_match(REAL_PDF, GOLDS))
      assert table["all"]["parser_exact"] == "3 of 3 (100%)"
      assert table["all"]["baseline_exact"] == "2 of 3 (67%)"
      assert table["repeated labels"]["parser_exact"] == "2 of 2 (100%)"  # the two その他 rows
      assert table["repeated labels"]["baseline_exact"] == "1 of 2 (50%)"
      assert table["nil and zero-with-mark"]["parser_exact"] == "0 of 0"  # no percentage on an empty slice
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short tests/test_report.py 2>&1 | tail -3
  cat > src/bsparser/report.py <<'EOF'
  from collections import Counter
  from typing import Dict, List
  
  from bsparser.gold import Gold, Result
  
  
  def _share(hits: int, total: int) -> str:
      """Always raw counts; a percentage only when there is something to divide by."""
      return f"{hits} of {total}" if total == 0 else f"{hits} of {total} ({round(100 * hits / total)}%)"
  
  
  def _slices(output, golds: List[Gold]) -> Dict[str, List[int]]:
      label_counts = Counter(r["label"] for r in output["rows"])
      marks = ("-", "△0")
      pick = {
          "all": lambda g: True,
          "repeated labels": lambda g: label_counts[g.label] > 1,
          "nil and zero-with-mark": lambda g: g.raw.replace("－", "-").replace(" ", "") in marks,
          "net and contra": lambda g: g.role in ("net", "contra"),
      }
      return {name: [i for i, g in enumerate(golds) if keep(g)] for name, keep in pick.items()}
  
  
  def summarise(output, golds: List[Gold], parser: List[Result], baseline: List[Result]) -> Dict[str, Dict[str, str]]:
      table = {}
      for name, idx in _slices(output, golds).items():
          wrong = [parser[i] for i in idx if parser[i].status != "exact"]
          table[name] = {
              "parser_exact": _share(sum(parser[i].status == "exact" for i in idx), len(idx)),
              "baseline_exact": _share(sum(baseline[i].status == "exact" for i in idx), len(idx)),
              "parser_silent_errors": _share(sum(not r.flagged for r in wrong), len(idx)),
              "parser_flagged_errors": _share(sum(r.flagged for r in wrong), len(idx)),
          }
      return table
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short 2>&1 | tail -3)

  cat >> src/bsparser/report.py <<'EOF'
  
  
  def render_markdown(title: str, table: Dict[str, Dict[str, str]]) -> str:
      lines = [f"### {title}", "",
               "| slice | parser (value + role) | parser (value only) | first-match baseline (value only) "
               "| parser silent errors | parser flagged errors |",
               "|---|---|---|---|---|---|"]
      for name, row in table.items():
          lines.append(f"| {name} | {row['parser_exact']} | {row['parser_value_exact']} | {row['baseline_exact']} "
                       f"| {row['parser_silent_errors']} | {row['parser_flagged_errors']} |")
      return "\n".join(lines) + "\n"
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short 2>&1 | tail -3)

  cat >> src/bsparser/gold.py <<'EOF'
  
  
  def gold_page(template_path):
      """The gold values as a Page, so the same identity checks can judge the gold itself."""
      from bsparser.amounts import parse_amount
      from bsparser.page import Page, Row
  
      periods: List[str] = []
      rows: List[Row] = []
      for line in Path(template_path).read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines():
          if line.startswith("| #"):
              periods = PERIOD_COLUMN.findall(line)
          elif re.match(r"^\| \d+ \|", line):
              cells = _cells(line)
              path = [p for p in cells[1].split("/") if p]
              role = cells[4]
              row = Row(id=f"r{cells[0]}", label=cells[2], level=len(path), role=role, path=path,
                        values=[parse_amount(raw) for raw in cells[6:6 + len(periods)] if raw])
              if role in ("contra", "net") and path:
                  gross = [r for r in rows if r.label == path[-1] and r.role == "item"]
                  row.parent = gross[-1].id if gross else None
              rows.append(row)
      return Page(rows=rows, periods=periods)
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short 2>&1 | tail -3)

⏺ Bash(cat > tests/test_accuracy.py <<'EOF'
  from pathlib import Path
  
  from bsparser.accuracy import accuracy_markdown
  
  ROOT = Path(__file__).parent.parent / "data"
  REAL_PDF = ROOT / "balance_sheet_after_correction.pdf"
  GOLD_TEMPLATE = ROOT / "gold" / "balance_sheet_after_correction.template.md"
  
  
  def test_accuracy_report_has_counts_baseline_errors_gold_checks_and_the_limits():
      text = accuracy_markdown(REAL_PDF, GOLD_TEMPLATE, "Japanese page")
      assert "| all | 74 of 74 (100%) | 74 of 74 (100%) | 60 of 74 (81%) |" in text
      assert "| repeated labels | 20 of 20 (100%) | 20 of 20 (100%) | 6 of 20 (30%) |" in text
      assert "Gold values pass the accounting identities: 20 of 20" in text
      assert "first-match baseline errors: 14 of 74" in text
      assert "read by a separate Claude session" in text
      assert "developed on this same page" in text
      assert "—" not in text  # style: no em dashes
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short tests/test_accuracy.py 2>&1 | tail -3
  cat > src/bsparser/accuracy.py <<'EOF'
  import sys
  from pathlib import Path
  
  from bsparser.baseline import first_match
  from bsparser.checks import run_checks
  from bsparser.gold import evaluate, gold_page, load_gold
  from bsparser.output import build_output
  from bsparser.report import render_markdown, summarise
  
  LIMITS = [
      "Gold values were read from the page image by a separate Claude session, not by a human, and not from the text "
      "layer or the parser. A reading error shared by two models is possible.",
      "The parser rules were developed on this same page, so a perfect score here is in-sample. It is not evidence "
      "about other filings. Held-out layouts (messy English variant, other filings) are still to be measured.",
      "The baseline is a deliberately naive lookup: first line with the same label, section path ignored.",
      "Gold is compared as printed text. The parser's own sign and nil rules are covered by separate unit tests.",
  ]
  
  
  def accuracy_markdown(pdf_path, template_path, title: str) -> str:
      golds = load_gold(template_path)
      out = build_output(pdf_path)
      parser = evaluate(out, golds)
      baseline = first_match(pdf_path, golds)
      gold_checks = run_checks(gold_page(template_path))
      gold_pass = sum(c.status == "pass" for c in gold_checks)
      wrong = [r for r in baseline if r.status != "exact"]
      lines = [render_markdown(f"{title}: {len(golds)} gold cells, page status {out['status']}",
                               summarise(out, golds, parser, baseline))]
      lines.append(f"Gold values pass the accounting identities: {gold_pass} of {len(gold_checks)}")
      lines.append("")
      lines.append(f"first-match baseline errors: {len(wrong)} of {len(golds)}")
      for r in wrong:
          lines.append(f"- {'/'.join(r.gold.path)} / {r.gold.label} / {r.gold.period} / gold {r.gold.raw}")
      lines.append("")
      lines.append("Limits:")
      lines.extend(f"- {text}" for text in LIMITS)
      return "\n".join(lines) + "\n"
  
  
  def main(argv) -> int:
      if len(argv) != 4:
          print("usage: python -m bsparser.accuracy <pdf> <gold template> <title> <out file>", file=sys.stderr)
          return 2
      Path(argv[3]).write_text(accuracy_markdown(argv[0], argv[1], argv[2]), encoding="utf-8")
      print(f"wrote {argv[3]}")
      return 0
  
  
  if __name__ == "__main__":
      sys.exit(main(sys.argv[1:]))
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short 2>&1 | tail -3)

  p = "/Users/teja/Desktop/ur-ai-parser-setup/src/bsparser/page.py"
  s = open(p, encoding="utf-8").read()
  s = s.replace("from bsparser.roles import classify_role\n", "from bsparser.roles import classify_role\nfrom bsparser.wrapped import TIGHT_RATIO\n")
  s = s.replace("def build_page(lines: List[Line]) -> Page:\n", '''def _join(first: str, second: str) -> str:
      cjk = re.compile("[\\u3040-\\u30ff\\u3400-\\u9fff]")
      return first + second if cjk.search(first[-1:]) or cjk.search(second[:1]) else f"{first} {second}"
  
  
  def _merge_wrapped(lines: List[Line]):
      """Merge a no-value line with the next line when both signals agree: much tighter spacing than usual,
      the same level or deeper, and the next line carries the values. Returns (lines, merged line positions)."""
      gaps = sorted(b.top - a.top for a, b in zip(lines, lines[1:]))
      usual = gaps[len(gaps) // 2] if gaps else 0.0
      merged_lines: List[Line] = []
      merged_at: List[int] = []
      i = 0
      while i < len(lines):
          line = lines[i]
          nxt = lines[i + 1] if i + 1 < len(lines) else None
          if (nxt is not None and not line.amounts and nxt.amounts
                  and nxt.top - line.top < TIGHT_RATIO * usual and nxt.x0 >= line.x0 - LEVEL_TOLERANCE):
              merged_at.append(len(merged_lines))
              merged_lines.append(Line(_join(line.label, nxt.label), line.x0, line.top, nxt.amounts))
              i += 2
          else:
              merged_lines.append(line)
              i += 1
      return merged_lines, merged_at
  
  
  def build_page(lines: List[Line]) -> Page:
      lines, merged_at = _merge_wrapped(lines)
  ''', 1)
  s = s.replace("    return Page(rows=rows, issues=issues)\n\n\ndef parse_page", '''    for position in merged_at:
          issues.append(Issue("wrapped_label_merged", rows[position].id,
                              f"label {rows[position].label!r} was wrapped over two lines and merged"))
      return Page(rows=rows, issues=issues)
  
  
  def parse_page''')
  open(p, "w", encoding="utf-8").write(s)
  EOF

⏺ Bash(cat >> tests/test_levels.py <<'EOF'
  
  
  def test_a_level_far_off_the_regular_step_is_irregular_and_a_regular_page_has_none():
      from bsparser.levels import irregular_levels
  
      regular = [60.0, 60.4, 72.1, 84.0, 83.7, 96.0, 108.2]
      assert irregular_levels(regular, tolerance=2.0) == []
      stray = [60.0, 72.0, 84.0, 90.0, 96.0, 108.0]  # 90 sits half a step off the 12-point grid
      assert irregular_levels(stray, tolerance=2.0) == [3]  # the level index of x = 90
      assert irregular_levels([60.0, 72.0], tolerance=2.0) == []  # too few levels to estimate a step
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short tests/test_levels.py 2>&1 | tail -3
  cat > src/bsparser/levels.py <<'EOF'
  from statistics import median
  from typing import List
  
  IRREGULAR_SHARE = 0.25  # off the grid by more than this share of a step (and more than the tolerance)
  
  
  def _clusters(xs: List[float], tolerance: float) -> List[List[float]]:
      """Left to right. A position within `tolerance` of the previous one joins its cluster."""
      clusters: List[List[float]] = []
      for x in sorted(xs):
          if clusters and x - clusters[-1][-1] <= tolerance:
              clusters[-1].append(x)
          else:
              clusters.append([x])
      return clusters
  
  
  def assign_levels(xs: List[float], tolerance: float) -> List[int]:
      """Level 0 is the leftmost cluster."""
      clusters = _clusters(xs, tolerance)
      return [next(i for i, c in enumerate(clusters) if x <= c[-1] + 1e-9) for x in xs]
  
  
  def irregular_levels(xs: List[float], tolerance: float) -> List[int]:
      """Levels whose centre is far from the regular grid (leftmost centre plus whole steps).
      The step is the median gap between level centres, so it needs at least three levels."""
      centres = [sum(c) / len(c) for c in _clusters(xs, tolerance)]
      if len(centres) < 3:
          return []
      step = median(b - a for a, b in zip(centres, centres[1:]))
      limit = max(tolerance, IRREGULAR_SHARE * step)
      irregular = []
      for i, centre in enumerate(centres):
          position = (centre - centres[0]) / step
          if abs(position - round(position)) * step > limit:
              irregular.append(i)
      return irregular
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short 2>&1 | tail -3)

⏺ Bash(cat > tests/test_vision.py <<'EOF'
  import json
  from pathlib import Path
  
  from bsparser.page import parse_lines, parse_page
  from bsparser.vision import lines_from_vision
  
  FIXTURES = Path(__file__).parent.parent / "data" / "fixtures"
  
  
  def canned_reply():
      """What a perfect vision model would return for the English fixture page."""
      data = json.loads((FIXTURES / "english_balance_sheet.json").read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
      return {
          "header": [f"({data['unit_raw']})", "(Mar. 31, 2025) (Mar. 31, 2026)"],
          "rows": [{"label": r["label"], "depth": r["depth"],
                    "amounts": [v["raw"] for v in r["values"]] if r["values"] else []}
                   for r in data["rows"]],
      }
  
  
  def test_a_vision_reply_becomes_the_same_page_as_the_text_parser_gives():
      vision = parse_lines(lines_from_vision(canned_reply()))
      text = parse_page(FIXTURES / "english_balance_sheet.pdf")
      assert [(r.label, r.role, r.level, r.parent) for r in vision.rows] == [
          (r.label, r.role, r.level, r.parent) for r in text.rows]
      assert [[(a.value, a.kind) for a in r.values] for r in vision.rows] == [
          [(a.value, a.kind) for a in r.values] for r in text.rows]
      assert (vision.periods, vision.unit, vision.issues) == (text.periods, text.unit, text.issues)
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short tests/test_vision.py 2>&1 | tail -3
  cat > src/bsparser/vision.py <<'EOF'
  from typing import Dict, List
  
  from bsparser.pdf_lines import Line
  
  LEFT, STEP, LINE_HEIGHT = 60.0, 12.0, 14.0  # synthetic geometry: the model gives depth, not pixels
  
  
  def lines_from_vision(reply: Dict) -> List[Line]:
      """Turn a vision reply into the same Line objects the text parser makes, so roles, checks and status are shared."""
      lines = [Line(text, LEFT, i * LINE_HEIGHT) for i, text in enumerate(reply["header"])]
      top = len(lines) * LINE_HEIGHT
      for row in reply["rows"]:
          lines.append(Line(row["label"], LEFT + STEP * row["depth"], top, list(row["amounts"])))
          top += LINE_HEIGHT
      return lines
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short 2>&1 | tail -3)

  def test_reply_text_with_code_fences_or_chatter_still_yields_the_json_and_garbage_is_an_error():
      import pytest
  
      from bsparser.vision import extract_reply
  
      payload = {"header": ["h"], "rows": []}
      raw = json.dumps(payload, ensure_ascii=False)
      assert extract_reply(raw) == payload
      assert extract_reply(f"Here is the table:\n```json\n{raw}\n```\nDone.") == payload
      with pytest.raises(ValueError):
          extract_reply("I could not read the page.")
      with pytest.raises(ValueError):
          extract_reply('{"header": ["h"]}')  # rows missing: wrong shape is never guessed
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short tests/test_vision.py 2>&1 | tail -3
  cat >> src/bsparser/vision.py <<'EOF'
  
  
  import json
  
  
  def extract_reply(text: str) -> Dict:
      """The JSON object in a model reply, with or without chatter or code fences. Wrong shape is an error."""
      start, end = text.find("{"), text.rfind("}")
      if start < 0 or end < start:
          raise ValueError("no JSON object in the vision reply")
      try:
          reply = json.loads(text[start:end + 1])
      except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
          raise ValueError(f"vision reply is not valid JSON: {exc}") from exc
      if not isinstance(reply.get("header"), list) or not isinstance(reply.get("rows"), list):
          raise ValueError("vision reply needs 'header' (list) and 'rows' (list)")
      return reply
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short 2>&1 | tail -3)

  def test_runner_renders_the_page_to_png_and_asks_headless_claude_to_read_only_the_image():
      from bsparser.vision import run_vision
  
      calls = []
  
      def fake_runner(cmd):
          png = [part for part in cmd if part.endswith(".png")] or [p for p in cmd[-1].split() if p.endswith(".png")]
          calls.append((cmd, Path(png[0]).exists()))
          return json.dumps(canned_reply(), ensure_ascii=False)
  
      reply = run_vision(FIXTURES / "english_balance_sheet.pdf", runner=fake_runner)
      (cmd, png_existed), = calls
      assert png_existed  # the image was rendered before the model was called
      assert cmd[0] == "claude" and "-p" in cmd
      assert cmd[cmd.index("--allowedTools") + 1] == "Read"  # nothing else: no shell, no edits
      prompt = cmd[-1]
      assert ".png" in prompt and "english_balance_sheet" not in prompt  # image path only, not the PDF or its text
      assert "exactly as printed" in prompt and "do not add, fix or compute" in prompt
      assert reply["rows"][0]["label"] == "Assets"
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short tests/test_vision.py 2>&1 | tail -3
  cat >> src/bsparser/vision.py <<'EOF'
  
  
  import subprocess
  import tempfile
  from pathlib import Path
  
  PROMPT = """Read the image file {png} (one page of a financial statement) and transcribe its table.
  Return ONLY one JSON object, no other text:
  {{"header": [<each text line above the table: unit line, period column headings>],
    "rows": [{{"label": <label exactly as printed>, "depth": <indent level, 0 for the leftmost labels>,
              "amounts": [<one string per period column exactly as printed, [] when the line has no numbers>]}}]}}
  Rules: copy every number exactly as printed, including marks such as a triangle, brackets, minus signs and dashes.
  A dash alone is a printed nil: keep it as the dash. Keep full-width characters as they are.
  A label wrapped over two lines is one row (join the two lines; the numbers belong to it).
  Keep every line in page order, including section headers with no numbers and totals.
  Do not add, fix or compute any value, and do not skip a line you cannot read: copy what you see."""
  
  
  def _claude_runner(cmd) -> str:
      return subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=300, check=True).stdout
  
  
  def run_vision(pdf_path, runner=_claude_runner, dpi: int = 150) -> Dict:
      """Render page 1 to a PNG and ask headless Claude Code (Read tool only) to transcribe it.
      Prototype: production would call an API or a fine-tuned model."""
      with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
          stem = Path(tmp) / "page"
          subprocess.run(["pdftoppm", "-r", str(dpi), "-f", "1", "-l", "1", "-png", "-singlefile", str(pdf_path), str(stem)],
                         check=True, capture_output=True)
          png = f"{stem}.png"
          cmd = ["claude", "-p", "--allowedTools", "Read", "--add-dir", tmp, PROMPT.format(png=png)]
          return extract_reply(runner(cmd))
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short 2>&1 | tail -3)

  p = "src/bsparser/output.py"

  import copy
  from pathlib import Path
  
  from bsparser.arbitrate import arbitrate
  from bsparser.output import build_output
  
  FIXTURE_PDF = Path(__file__).parent.parent / "data" / "fixtures" / "english_balance_sheet.pdf"
  
  
  def break_a_total(output):
      """A copy where one printed total is far off, so a check fails and the page is failed."""
      broken = copy.deepcopy(output)
      for r in broken["records"]:
          if r["label"] == "Total current assets" and r["period"] == "2026-03-31":
              r["value"] += 100
      for c in broken["checks"]:
          if c["label"] == "Total current assets" and c["period"] == "2026-03-31":
              c["difference"] += 100
              c["status"] = "failed"
      broken["status"] = "failed"
      return broken
  
  
  def test_the_output_with_fewer_failed_checks_wins_and_the_choice_is_recorded():
      good = build_output(FIXTURE_PDF)
      bad = break_a_total(good)
      chosen = arbitrate(text=bad, vision=good)
      assert chosen["extractor"] == "vision" and chosen["status"] == "ok"
      assert chosen["alternative"]["extractor"] == "text"  # both outputs are kept
      assert [i["kind"] for i in chosen["issues"]] == ["extractor_chosen"]
      assert "vision" in chosen["issues"][0]["message"] and "failed" in chosen["issues"][0]["message"]
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short tests/test_arbitrate.py 2>&1 | tail -3
  cat > src/bsparser/arbitrate.py <<'EOF'
  import copy
  from typing import Dict
  
  
  def _score(output: Dict):
      """Lower is better: failed checks first, then skipped checks and unreadable values."""
      failed = sum(c["status"] == "failed" for c in output["checks"])
      doubtful = sum(c["status"] == "skipped" for c in output["checks"]) + sum(
          r["kind"] == "unreadable" for r in output["records"])
      return (failed, doubtful)
  
  
  def arbitrate(text: Dict, vision: Dict) -> Dict:
      """D7: better check result wins, a tie goes to text. No cell-level merging. Both outputs are kept."""
      text, vision = copy.deepcopy(text), copy.deepcopy(vision)
      text["extractor"], vision["extractor"] = "text", "vision"
      winner, loser = (vision, text) if _score(vision) < _score(text) else (text, vision)
      winner["alternative"] = loser
      winner["issues"].append({
          "kind": "extractor_chosen", "row_id": "",
          "message": f"{winner['extractor']} chosen: failed/doubtful checks {_score(winner)} against "
                     f"{loser['extractor']} {_score(loser)} (failed, skipped or unreadable)"})
      return winner
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short 2>&1 | tail -3)
