  p = "src/bsparser/analyse.py"
  s = open(p, encoding="utf-8").read()
  s = s.replace("from bsparser.rounding import find_rounding_hint\n", "from bsparser.rounding import find_rounding_hint\nfrom bsparser.statement import cross_checks\n", 1)
  s = s.replace("import tempfile\n", "import re\nimport tempfile\n", 1)
  old = s[s.index("def analyse("):]
  new = '''def _page_output(pdf_path, page: int, pages_total: int, mode: str, source: str, sentence: str, note: str,
                   parsed=None) -> Dict:
      if parsed is None:
          one_page = extract_page(pdf_path, page, Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="bsparser_")) / "page.pdf")
          parsed = parse_page(one_page)
      out = output_from_page(parsed, str(pdf_path), rounding=mode, rounding_source=source,
                             rounding_sentence=sentence, rounding_note=note)
      out["page"], out["pages_total"] = page, pages_total
      return out
  
  
  def _parse(pdf_path, page: int):
      return parse_page(extract_page(pdf_path, page, Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="bsparser_")) / "page.pdf"))
  
  
  def _resolve_rounding(pdf_path, unit, rounding: str):
      if rounding == "auto":
          hint = find_rounding_hint(pdf_path, unit)
          return hint.mode, hint.source, hint.sentence, hint.note
      return rounding, "user", "", ""
  
  
  def analyse(pdf_path, page: Optional[int] = None, rounding: str = "auto") -> Dict:
      """One path for the command line, the held-out script and the page, so no result depends on hand-picked options.
      Finds the balance sheet page (unless given), parses it with the parser unchanged, and sets the rounding mode:
      `auto` takes the cover hint (D18), an explicit value is recorded as chosen by the user."""
      with pdfplumber.open(pdf_path) as pdf:
          pages_total = len(pdf.pages)
      if page is None:
          candidates = find_balance_sheet_pages(pdf_path)
          page = candidates[0].page if candidates else 1
      parsed = _parse(pdf_path, page)
      mode, source, sentence, note = _resolve_rounding(pdf_path, parsed.unit, rounding)
      return _page_output(pdf_path, page, pages_total, mode, source, sentence, note, parsed=parsed)
  
  
  CONTINUES = re.compile(r"負債の部|純資産の部|負債合計|純資産合計|負債純資産合計|total liabilities|total net assets|total equity", re.I)
  MAX_FOLLOWING = 2
  RANK = {"ok": 0, "failed": 1, "unreadable": 2}
  
  
  def analyse_statement(pdf_path, page: Optional[int] = None, rounding: str = "auto") -> Dict:
      """Q23. The assets page plus the following pages (up to 2) with the same unit and periods that carry liabilities
      or net assets wording, each parsed page by page with the parser unchanged, then checked across pages.
      The rounding mode is resolved once, from the assets page, so every page uses the same one."""
      with pdfplumber.open(pdf_path) as pdf:
          pages_total = len(pdf.pages)
          texts = {n: pdf.pages[n - 1].extract_text() or "" for n in range(1, pages_total + 1)}
      if page is None:
          candidates = find_balance_sheet_pages(pdf_path)
          page = candidates[0].page if candidates else 1
      first = _parse(pdf_path, page)
      mode, source, sentence, note = _resolve_rounding(pdf_path, first.unit, rounding)
      outputs = [_page_output(pdf_path, page, pages_total, mode, source, sentence, note, parsed=first)]
      for nxt in range(page + 1, min(page + MAX_FOLLOWING, pages_total) + 1):
          if not CONTINUES.search(texts[nxt]):
              break
          parsed = _parse(pdf_path, nxt)
          if (parsed.unit, parsed.periods) != (first.unit, first.periods):
              break
          outputs.append(_page_output(pdf_path, nxt, pages_total, mode, source, sentence, note, parsed=parsed))
      cross = cross_checks(outputs, mode)
      worst = max([o["status"] for o in outputs] + (["failed"] if any(c["status"] == "failed" for c in cross) else []),
                  key=RANK.get)
      return {"source": str(pdf_path), "status": worst, "rounding": mode, "rounding_source": source,
              "rounding_sentence": sentence, "rounding_note": note, "pages": outputs, "cross_checks": cross}
  '''
  s = s.replace(old, new)
  open(p, "w", encoding="utf-8").write(s)
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short 2>&1 | tail -4)

  def write_statement_outputs(statement, out_dir):
      """A statement spans several pages: one JSON (pages, cross-page checks, status) and one CSV of all records."""
      out_dir = Path(out_dir)
      out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
      stem = Path(statement["source"]).stem
      json_path = out_dir / f"{stem}.statement.json"
      json_path.write_text(json.dumps(statement, ensure_ascii=False, indent=1), encoding="utf-8")
      csv_path = out_dir / f"{stem}.statement.records.csv"
      with csv_path.open("w", encoding="utf-8", newline="") as f:
          writer = csv.DictWriter(f, fieldnames=["page"] + RECORD_COLUMNS)
          writer.writeheader()
          for page in statement["pages"]:
              for rec in page["records"]:
                  row = dict(rec)
                  row["page"] = page["page"]
                  row["path"] = " / ".join(rec["path"])
                  row["value"] = "" if rec["value"] is None else rec["value"]
                  row["parent"] = rec["parent"] or ""
                  row["warnings"] = "; ".join(rec["warnings"])
                  row["notes"] = "; ".join(rec["notes"])
                  writer.writerow(row)
      return json_path, csv_path
  '''
  open(p, "w", encoding="utf-8").write(s)

  ⎿  def main() -> None:
         import pandas as pd
         import streamlit as st
     
         st.set_page_config(page_title="Balance sheet parser", layout="wide")
         st.title("Balance sheet parser")
         st.caption("Drop in a PDF. The page that looks like a balance sheet is found, parsed by the text layer "
                    "(no OCR, no vision model here), and checked with accounting identities.")
         upload = st.file_uploader("PDF", type="pdf")
         if upload is None:
             return
         work = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="bsparser_"))
         source = work / "upload.pdf"
         source.write_bytes(upload.getvalue())
         with pdfplumber.open(source) as pdf:
             total_pages = len(pdf.pages)
     
         candidates = find_balance_sheet_pages(source)
         if candidates:
             labels = [f"page {c.page}: {c.reason}" for c in candidates]
             choice = st.selectbox(f"{total_pages} pages. Balance sheet candidates, best first", labels)
             page = candidates[labels.index(choice)].page
         else:
             st.warning("No page looks like a balance sheet (no keywords such as 貸借対照表 or Total assets). Pick a page to try anyway.")
             page = st.number_input("Page", min_value=1, max_value=total_pages, value=1, step=1)
         page = int(page)
     
         choice = st.selectbox("Rounding of the printed amounts (sets the tolerance of the checks)",
                               ["auto (from the cover page)", "round", "cut", "unstated"])
         output = analyse(source, page=page, rounding="auto" if choice.startswith("auto") else choice)
         named = work / f"{Path(upload.name).stem}_p{page}.pdf"  # only its name is used, for the downloads
         json_path, csv_path = write_outputs(named, work / "out", output=output)
         origin = {"cover_sentence": "from the cover sentence", "user": "chosen by you", "unstated": "not stated"}
         st.info(f"Rounding: {output['rounding']} ({output['rounding_source']}, {origin[output['rounding_source']]}). "
                 f"{output['rounding_sentence'] or output['rounding_note']}")
     
         st.subheader(f"Status: {output['status']}")
         {"ok": st.success, "failed": st.error, "unreadable": st.warning}[output["status"]](STATUS_TEXT[output["status"]])
         left, mid, right = st.columns(3)
         left.metric("Unit", f"{output['unit'] or 'not found'}", output["unit_raw"] or None)
         mid.metric("Periods", ", ".join(output["periods"]) or "not found")
         right.metric("Rows / records", f"{len(output['rows'])} / {len(output['records'])}")
     
         if not output["rows"]:
             st.error("No table rows were found on this page. The parser needs a text layer; a scanned page has none.")
         else:
             st.subheader("Table")
             st.dataframe(pd.DataFrame(table_rows(output)), hide_index=True, width="stretch")
     
         st.subheader(f"Checks ({len(output['checks'])})")
         if output["checks"]:
             shown = pd.DataFrame(output["checks"]).drop(columns=["row_id"])
             shown.insert(0, "result", shown.pop("status").map({"pass": "pass", "failed": "FAILED", "skipped": "skipped"}))
             st.dataframe(shown, hide_index=True, width="stretch")
         else:
             st.info("No identity checks could be run on this page.")
     
         st.subheader(f"Issues ({len(output['issues'])})")
         if output["issues"]:
             st.dataframe(pd.DataFrame(output["issues"]), hide_index=True, width="stretch")
         else:
             st.write("None.")
     
         down_csv, down_json = st.columns(2)
         down_csv.download_button("Download records CSV", csv_path.read_bytes(), file_name=csv_path.name, mime="text/csv")
         down_json.download_button("Download full JSON", json_path.read_bytes(), file_name=json_path.name, mime="application/json")
     
     
     if __name__ == "__main__":
         main()

⏺ Bash(git add app.py tests && git commit -q -m "$(cat <<'EOF'
  feat: the page reads the following pages of the statement and shows cross-page checks
  
  Co-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
  EOF
  )" && cat > scripts/run_heldout.py <<'EOF'
  """Run every PDF in data/heldout through the same path as the page and the command line: find the balance sheet
  page, read the following pages of the statement, set the rounding mode from the cover hint, parse with the parser
  unchanged, run the page checks and the cross-page checks. Nothing is picked by hand.
  
  Run from the repo root: .venv/bin/python scripts/run_heldout.py <out_dir>
  """
  import sys
  from collections import Counter
  from pathlib import Path
  
  sys.path.insert(0, ".")
  sys.path.insert(0, "src")
  from bsparser.analyse import analyse_statement  # noqa: E402
  from bsparser.output import write_statement_outputs  # noqa: E402
  
  out_dir = Path(sys.argv[1])
  for pdf in sorted(Path("data/heldout").glob("*.pdf")):
      statement = analyse_statement(pdf)
      json_path, _ = write_statement_outputs(statement, out_dir)
      print("=" * 100)
      print(f"{pdf.name}: statement {statement['status']} | pages {[p['page'] for p in statement['pages']]} | "
            f"rounding {statement['rounding']} ({statement['rounding_source']})")
      if statement["rounding_sentence"] or statement["rounding_note"]:
          print("  ", statement["rounding_sentence"] or statement["rounding_note"])
      for page in statement["pages"]:
          counts = Counter(c["status"] for c in page["checks"])
          print(f"  page {page['page']}: {page['status']} unit={page['unit']} rows={len(page['rows'])} "
                f"checks={dict(counts)} issues={Counter(i['kind'] for i in page['issues'])}")
          for c in page["checks"]:
              if c["status"] != "pass":
                  print("     check:", c["status"], c["label"], c["period"], "diff", c["difference"], "bound", c["bound"], c["reason"])
      cross = Counter(c["status"] for c in statement["cross_checks"])
      print(f"  cross-page checks: {dict(cross)}")
      for c in statement["cross_checks"]:
          print(f"     {c['status']:7s} {c['period']} {c['label']} printed={c['printed']} expected={c['expected']} diff={c['difference']} bound={c['bound']}")
  EOF
  rm -rf outputs/heldout_after && .venv/bin/python scripts/run_heldout.py outputs/heldout_after 2>&1 | cut -c1-200)

  ⎿  - **The page is in-sample.** Every parser rule was developed against this one page, so 100% here is not evidence about other filings. The messy English variant is a held-out layout, but I built it myself. Real held-out filings are the missing test.
     - **Comparison is on printed text.** The parser's own sign and nil rules are covered by unit tests, not by gold. A wrong rule that also appears in the gold typing would not be caught.
     - **Scope.** One page, text-layer PDF, assets half only. Out of scope and flagged where cheap: multi-page tables, liabilities and equity on another page, other statements, merged cells. Eras other than Reiwa are flagged, not converted. A Japanese label missing from the translation dictionary gets `label_en: null` and a warning.
     - **Strict tolerance can hide real errors** of one or two units on small sections. It is reported with every check so the reader can see the margin.

⏺ All of D17 to D21 is built, one failing test at a time, and committed after each fix. The four held-out filings now pass end to end, and 165 tests pass. Here is what changed, in the order we agreed:
  
  1. **Crashes (first):** a nil printed total, a row with fewer values than periods, and a nil net or gross line each skip the check with a reason. An unexpected error inside a check also becomes a skipped check carrying the error text. The three crashing pages now return `unreadable` with every check skipped.
  2. **D17:** an aggregate 減価償却累計額 line has no parent and counts in the section sum.
  3. **D18:** the rounding setting (`round`, `cut`, `unstated`) works with the cover hint, which only counts when the cut-off unit equals the balance sheet unit. The source is recorded, and the same code runs in the page, the held-out script and the command line (`--rounding`, `--page`).
  4. **D19:** stand-alone `（純額）` lines are items with a note, in a separate `notes` field that never affects status or counts as a warning.
  5. **D20:** a last-line page number like `-5-` is left out.
  6. **Q22:** the group check. The bank page goes from 4 checks to 8.
  7. **D21:**
     - Total liabilities and net assets is checked as liabilities plus net assets.
     - A total with nothing to add up is skipped, never failed.
     - Headingless subtotals add only their run, and the final total adds the earlier subtotals.
     - The long bar, en dash and minus sign alone are nil.
     - Total labels are matched by exact wording.
     - The cross-page checks and `--statement` mode are in, and the page has a checkbox for the statement mode.
  
  **The "after" table** is in `docs/report.md`, and the raw runs are in `outputs/heldout_after/`:
  
  | file | pages | status | cross-page checks | rounding |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | 4098 | 6, 7 | ok | 4 of 4 | cut (cover) |
  | 4224 | 7, 8 | ok | 4 of 4 | cut (cover) |
  | 2452 | 6, 7 | ok | 4 of 4 | unstated (unit mismatch) |
  | bank | 5, 6 | ok | 4 of 4 | cut (cover) |
  
  Total assets equals total liabilities and net assets, exactly, on all four. The original page is unchanged at 74 of 74 against the baseline's 60 of 74.

They still have no gold, so `ok` means the totals hold, not that every cell is right. 2452 passes under the strict bound, with one cross-page difference exactly on it. All real filings tested are Japanese 決算短信 balance sheets.

⏺ Before I build, I want facts. Statement mode is already on by default in the app (the checkbox starts ticked, and a test checks it). So if you saw it off, that was an older version. The page-offering rule is a real design decision, so I'll look at what the finder offers today on your four filings and the original page, and what a stricter rule would keep.

⏺ Bash(.venv/bin/python -I - <<'EOF'
  import sys, re, unicodedata
  sys.path.insert(0, "src")
  import pdfplumber
  from bsparser.pages import find_balance_sheet_pages
  AMOUNT = re.compile(r"\d{1,3}(?:,\s?\d{3})+")
  files = ["data/balance_sheet_after_correction.pdf", "data/fixtures/english_balance_sheet.pdf"] + [f"data/heldout/{n}" for n in ("140120260416505142.pdf","140120260513529466.pdf","140120260514535920.pdf","tanshin202603.pdf")]
  for f in files:
      cands = {c.page: c for c in find_balance_sheet_pages(f)}
      print("==", f.split("/")[-1], "| offered today:", [(p, round(c.score,1)) for p, c in cands.items()])
      with pdfplumber.open(f) as pdf:
          for n, page in enumerate(pdf.pages, start=1):
              if n not in cands: continue
              t = unicodedata.normalize("NFKC", page.extract_text() or "")
              title = bool(re.search(r"貸借対照表|balance sheet|financial position", t, re.I))
              assets = bool(re.search(r"資産の部|total assets", t, re.I))
              total_assets = bool(re.search(r"(?m)^\s*(資産合計|資産の部合計)\b|total assets", t, re.I))
              liab = bool(re.search(r"負債の部|純資産の部|負債合計|純資産合計|total liabilities|total net assets", t, re.I))
              first = t.strip().splitlines()[0][:34] if t.strip() else ""
              print(f"   p{n:<2} title={title!s:5} assets_part={assets!s:5} total_assets={total_assets!s:5} liab_words={liab!s:5} amounts={len(AMOUNT.findall(t)):3} | {first}")
  EOF)

⏺ Here are the facts behind the rule. The finder offers 5 to 13 pages today because one keyword is enough. A balance-sheet start page in these files has three things at once: a balance-sheet title, an assets part, and a total-assets line. That rule keeps exactly the right page in 4098 (page 6), 4224 (page 7), 2452 (page 6) and the original page. In the bank it keeps page 5 and page 14. Page 14 is a second, genuine balance sheet, the parent-only one (個別貸借対照表), so it should stay offered. It correctly drops 4224's per-share page, the segment page in 2452, and the equity statement. The liabilities pages are not offered separately, because statement mode reads them together with the assets page.
  
  ❓ **Q24 - What counts as a real balance sheet, and what else to build**: I suggest the following.
  
  - **Pages offered:** a page is offered only when it has all three of a balance-sheet title (貸借対照表, balance sheet, financial position), an assets part (資産の部 or Total assets), and a total-assets line (資産合計, 資産の部合計, Total assets). Each offered page shows its heading, so the bank's two statements read as 連結 and 個別. There is an expander "My page isn't listed" that allows any page, so a false negative never blocks you.
  - **Statement mode:** it is already on by default. I'd keep the checkbox, because a standalone balance sheet should still be readable alone, and move it under the page choice.
  - **Page image:** each page's image sits to the left of its table, with the table and checks to the right.
  - **Highlighting:** rows in a failed check get a red background. That means the printed total and the lines it adds up. For a failed cross-page check, the two totals are highlighted on their pages. Skipped checks are not highlighted. Each row also gets a "why" column naming the check.
  - **Friendly messages:** no red error boxes and no tracebacks. Plain-language messages with a next step cover these cases: not a readable PDF, no balance sheet page found, a page without a text layer (scanned: "this looks like an image, the vision fallback is not available in this page"), and `unreadable` or `failed` statuses, each with what it means and what to look at.
  
  ➡️ Yes. Offer only pages with title, assets part and total-assets line (heading shown, any-page expander as an escape), checkbox kept under the page choice, page image beside each table, failed-check rows in red with a "why" column, and friendly messages with next steps for every error case.

║ Yes, sounds good. Go ahead.

⏺ The spec is agreed, so I'm starting. The order is the page finder first, then which rows belong to each check, then the page image, highlighting, friendly messages, and the layout last. Slice 1 is the strict finder. The test uses your four filings: it must offer exactly 4098 page 6, 4224 page 7, 2452 page 6, and the bank's pages 5 and 14. Each offered page carries a heading, so the bank's two statements can be told apart.

⏺ Bash(cat > tests/test_pages.py <<'EOF'
  from pathlib import Path
  
  import pytest
  
  from bsparser.pages import find_balance_sheet_pages
  
  ROOT = Path(__file__).parent.parent
  HELDOUT = ROOT / "data" / "heldout"
  
  
  @pytest.mark.parametrize("name,offered", [
      ("140120260416505142.pdf", [6]),
      ("140120260513529466.pdf", [7]),
      ("140120260514535920.pdf", [6]),
      ("tanshin202603.pdf", [5, 14]),  # consolidated and parent-only balance sheets
  ])
  def test_only_pages_with_a_title_an_assets_part_and_a_total_assets_line_are_offered(name, offered):
      assert sorted(c.page for c in find_balance_sheet_pages(HELDOUT / name)) == offered
  
  
  def test_each_offered_page_shows_a_heading_that_tells_consolidated_from_parent_only():
      bank = {c.page: c.heading for c in find_balance_sheet_pages(HELDOUT / "tanshin202603.pdf")}
      assert "連結貸借対照表" in bank[5] and "個別" not in bank[5]
      assert "個別財務諸表" in bank[14] and "貸借対照表" in bank[14]
  
  
  def test_the_loose_list_is_still_available_for_the_my_page_isnt_listed_escape():
      loose = find_balance_sheet_pages(HELDOUT / "140120260416505142.pdf", strict=False)
      assert {6, 7, 11}.issubset({c.page for c in loose}) and len(loose) > 1
  EOF

     KEYWORDS = [  # (text, lowercase match, score)
         ("貸借対照表", False, 3), ("資産の部", False, 3), ("資産合計", False, 3),
         ("balance sheet", True, 3), ("total assets", True, 3), ("financial position", True, 2),
     ]
     
     
     @dataclass
     class Candidate:
         page: int  # 1-based
         score: float
         reason: str
     
     
     def find_balance_sheet_pages(pdf_path) -> List[Candidate]:
         """Pages that look like a balance sheet, best first. Keyword hits plus how many thousands-grouped numbers it has."""
         found = []
         with pdfplumber.open(pdf_path) as pdf:
             for number, page in enumerate(pdf.pages, start=1):
                 text = page.extract_text() or ""
                 hits = [word for word, lower, _ in KEYWORDS if (word in text.lower() if lower else word in text)]
                 if not hits:
                     continue
                 score = sum(s for word, _, s in KEYWORDS if word in hits)
                 amounts = len(AMOUNT.findall(text))
                 score += min(amounts, 20) / 10
                 found.append(Candidate(number, score, f"matches: {', '.join(hits)}; {amounts} grouped numbers"))
         return sorted(found, key=lambda c: (-c.score, c.page))
     
     
     def extract_page(pdf_path, page: int, out_path) -> Path:
         """Write one page of a PDF to its own file, so the parser (which reads page 1) can be used unchanged."""
         subprocess.run(["pdfseparate", "-f", str(page), "-l", str(page), str(pdf_path), str(out_path)],
                        check=True, capture_output=True)
         return Path(out_path)

⏺ Bash(python3 - <<'EOF'
  p = "src/bsparser/pages.py"
  s = open(p, encoding="utf-8").read()
  s = s.replace("import re\nimport subprocess\n", "import re\nimport subprocess\nimport unicodedata\n", 1)
  i = s.index("@dataclass\nclass Candidate:")
  j = s.index("def extract_page")
  new = '''TITLE = re.compile(r"貸借対照表|balance sheet|financial position", re.I)
  ASSETS_PART = re.compile(r"資産の部|total assets|^\\s*assets\\s*$", re.I | re.M)
  TOTAL_ASSETS_LINE = re.compile(r"^\\s*(?:資産合計|資産の部合計|total assets)", re.I | re.M)
  HEADING_WORDS = re.compile(r"財務諸表|financial statements|貸借対照表|balance sheet|financial position", re.I)
  
  
  @dataclass
  class Candidate:
      page: int  # 1-based
      score: float
      reason: str
      heading: str = ""  # e.g. "4. 個別財務諸表 / (1) 貸借対照表", so consolidated and parent-only statements read apart
  
  
  def _heading(text: str) -> str:
      lines = [line.strip() for line in text.splitlines()[:8] if HEADING_WORDS.search(line)]
      return " / ".join(lines[:2])
  
  
  def find_balance_sheet_pages(pdf_path, strict: bool = True) -> List[Candidate]:
      """Pages that look like a balance sheet, best first.
      strict (the default): the page has a balance-sheet title, an assets part and a total-assets line, all three.
      Liabilities pages are not offered: they are read together with their assets page (statement mode).
      strict=False: any page with a keyword, for the 'my page is not listed' escape."""
      found = []
      with pdfplumber.open(pdf_path) as pdf:
          for number, page in enumerate(pdf.pages, start=1):
              raw = page.extract_text() or ""
              text = unicodedata.normalize("NFKC", raw)
              if strict and not (TITLE.search(text) and ASSETS_PART.search(text) and TOTAL_ASSETS_LINE.search(text)):
                  continue
              hits = [word for word, lower, _ in KEYWORDS if (word in text.lower() if lower else word in text)]
              if not hits:
                  continue
              score = sum(s for word, _, s in KEYWORDS if word in hits)
              amounts = len(AMOUNT.findall(text))
              score += min(amounts, 20) / 10
              found.append(Candidate(number, score, f"matches: {', '.join(hits)}; {amounts} grouped numbers", _heading(raw)))
      return sorted(found, key=lambda c: (-c.score, c.page))
  
  
  '''
  s = s[:i] + new + s[j:]
  open(p, "w", encoding="utf-8").write(s)
  EOF

  p = "src/bsparser/checks.py"

  s = s.replace('''def _skip_all(page: Page, row, reason: str) -> List[Check]:
      return [Check(row.label, row.id, period, None, None, None, 0, 0, "skipped", reason) for period in page.periods]
  ''', '''def _skip_all(page: Page, row, reason: str) -> List[Check]:
      return [Check(row.label, row.id, period, None, None, None, 0, 0, "skipped", reason) for period in page.periods]
  
  
  def _tag(checks: List[Check], rows) -> List[Check]:
      ids = [r.id for r in rows]
      for c in checks:
          c.row_ids = list(ids)
      return checks
  ''', 1)
  # net
  s = s.replace('''def _net_checks(page: Page, row, by_id, rounding: str) -> List[Check]:
      checks: List[Check] = []
      contra = [r for r in page.rows if r.role == "contra" and r.parent == row.parent]
      gross = by_id[row.parent]
      reason = _wrong_width([gross, row] + contra, page)
      if reason:
          return _skip_all(page, row, reason)
  ''', '''def _net_checks(page: Page, row, by_id, rounding: str) -> List[Check]:
      contra = [r for r in page.rows if r.role == "contra" and r.parent == row.parent]
      gross = by_id[row.parent]
      return _tag(_net_checks_untagged(page, row, gross, contra, rounding), [row, gross] + contra)
  
  
  def _net_checks_untagged(page: Page, row, gross, contra, rounding: str) -> List[Check]:
      checks: List[Check] = []
      reason = _wrong_width([gross, row] + contra, page)
      if reason:
          return _skip_all(page, row, reason)
  ''', 1)
  open(p, "w", encoding="utf-8").write(s)

  ⎿  def _la_na_checks(page: Page, row, rounding: str) -> List[Check]:
         pieces = {kind: [r for r in page.rows if r.values and kind_of_total(r.label) == kind] for kind in ("liabilities", "net_assets")}
         missing = [name for name, kind in (("liabilities", "liabilities"), ("net assets", "net_assets")) if not pieces[kind]]
         if missing:
             return _skip_all(page, row, "total " + " and total ".join(missing) + " not on this page")
         return _sum_of_totals(page, row, [pieces["liabilities"][0], pieces["net_assets"][0]], rounding)
     
     
     def _total_checks(page: Page, row, rounding: str) -> List[Check]:
         if kind_of_total(row.label) == "liabilities_net_assets":
             return _la_na_checks(page, row, rounding)
         checks: List[Check] = []
         contributors = _contributors(page, row)
         if not contributors:
             return _skip_all(page, row, "no components on this page")  # never a failure for lack of components
         reason = _wrong_width(contributors + [row], page)
         if reason:
             return _skip_all(page, row, reason)
         for p, period in enumerate(page.periods):
             unreadable = [r for r in contributors + [row] if r.values[p].kind == "unreadable"]
             if unreadable:
                 ids = ", ".join(r.id for r in unreadable)
                 checks.append(Check(row.label, row.id, period, None, None, None, 0, 0, "skipped",
                                     f"unreadable value in {ids}"))
                 continue
             printed = row.values[p].value
             if printed is None:
                 checks.append(Check(row.label, row.id, period, None, None, None, 0, 0, "skipped",
                                     f"printed total is nil in {row.id}"))
                 continue
             amounts = [c.values[p].value for c in contributors if c.values[p].value is not None]
             expected = sum(amounts)
             checks.append(Check(row.label, row.id, period, expected, printed, printed - expected,
                                 bound_for(len(amounts), rounding), len(amounts),
                                 _status(printed - expected, bound_for(len(amounts), rounding))))
         return checks
     
     
     def _group_checks(page: Page, row, rounding: str) -> List[Check]:
         """Q22: an item that has its own value and indented detail lines directly below it equals the sum of them."""
         children = [r for r in page.rows if r.role == "item" and r.values and r.path == row.path + [row.label]]
         if not children or any(is_uchi_label(c.label) for c in children):
             return []  # nothing to add up, or the detail is a partial breakdown (うち / of which)
         reason = _wrong_width(children + [row], page)
         if reason:
             return _skip_all(page, row, reason)
         checks: List[Check] = []
         for p, period in enumerate(page.periods):
             unreadable = [r for r in children + [row] if r.values[p].kind == "unreadable"]
             if unreadable:
                 ids = ", ".join(r.id for r in unreadable)
                 checks.append(Check(row.label, row.id, period, None, None, None, 0, 0, "skipped", f"unreadable value in {ids}"))
                 continue
             printed = row.values[p].value
             if printed is None:
                 checks.append(Check(row.label, row.id, period, None, None, None, 0, 0, "skipped", f"printed total is nil in {row.id}"))
                 continue
             amounts = [c.values[p].value for c in children if c.values[p].value is not None]
             expected = sum(amounts)
             checks.append(Check(row.label, row.id, period, expected, printed, printed - expected,
                                 bound_for(len(amounts), rounding), len(amounts),
                                 _status(printed - expected, bound_for(len(amounts), rounding))))
         return checks
     
     
     def _guarded(page: Page, row, make) -> List[Check]:

⏺ Slice 3, the page image. I'm adding a function that renders one page to PNG bytes for the page to show beside the table. The test checks that the bytes really are a PNG with a sensible size.

⏺ Bash(git add src tests && git commit -q -m "feat: cross-page checks name the page and row of every total they used" && cat >> tests/test_pages.py <<'EOF'
  
  
  def test_a_page_renders_to_png_bytes_with_a_sensible_size_and_a_bad_page_is_an_error_not_an_image():
      import struct
  
      from bsparser.pages import render_page_png
  
      png = render_page_png(HELDOUT / "tanshin202603.pdf", 5, dpi=60)
      assert png[:8] == b"\x89PNG\r\n\x1a\n"
      width, height = struct.unpack(">II", png[16:24])
      assert 300 < width < 600 and 400 < height < 800  # A4 at 60 dpi is about 496 x 702
      with pytest.raises(ValueError):
          render_page_png(HELDOUT / "tanshin202603.pdf", 999)
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short tests/test_pages.py 2>&1 | tail -3
  cat >> src/bsparser/pages.py <<'EOF'
  
  
  def render_page_png(pdf_path, page: int, dpi: int = 100) -> bytes:
      """One page as PNG bytes, to show the page next to the parsed table."""
      import tempfile
  
      with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(prefix="bsparser_img_") as tmp:
          stem = Path(tmp) / "page"
          done = subprocess.run(["pdftoppm", "-r", str(dpi), "-f", str(page), "-l", str(page), "-png", "-singlefile",
                                 str(pdf_path), str(stem)], capture_output=True)
          png = Path(f"{stem}.png")
          if done.returncode != 0 or not png.exists():
              raise ValueError(f"page {page} could not be rendered: {done.stderr.decode(errors='replace').strip()[:200]}")
          return png.read_bytes()
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short tests/test_pages.py 2>&1 | tail -3)

  def failing_output(total_prior="602"):
      from bsparser.output import output_from_page
      from bsparser.page import parse_lines
      from bsparser.pdf_lines import Line
  
      page = parse_lines([
          Line("(in thousands of yen)", 400.0, 70.0),
          Line("(Mar. 31, 2025) (Mar. 31, 2026)", 300.0, 94.0),
          Line("Assets", 60.0, 120.0),
          Line("Current assets", 72.0, 134.0),
          Line("Cash", 84.0, 148.0, ["100", "100"]),
          Line("Notes receivable", 84.0, 162.0, ["200", "200"]),
          Line("Other", 84.0, 176.0, ["300", "300"]),
          Line("Total current assets", 84.0, 190.0, [total_prior, "600"]),  # 2025: 3 items, bound 2, 602 passes, 604 fails
          Line("Land", 72.0, 204.0, ["50", "50"]),
          Line("Total assets", 72.0, 218.0, ["654", "650"]),  # 604 + 50 in 2025
      ])
      out = output_from_page(page, "synthetic.pdf")
      out["page"] = 1
      return out
  
  
  def test_rows_of_a_failed_check_are_marked_with_why_and_skipped_or_passing_checks_mark_nothing():
      from app import failed_rows, table_rows
  
      ok = failing_output("602")
      assert failed_rows(ok) == {}
      bad = failing_output("605")  # 605 against 600: difference 5, bound 2
      why = failed_rows(bad)
      ids = {r["label"]: r["id"] for r in bad["rows"]}
      assert set(why) == {ids["Total current assets"], ids["Cash"], ids["Notes receivable"], ids["Other"]} | {
          ids["Total assets"], ids["Land"]} or set(why) >= {ids["Total current assets"], ids["Cash"], ids["Notes receivable"], ids["Other"]}
      assert "Total current assets" in why[ids["Cash"]][0] and "2025-03-31" in why[ids["Cash"]][0] and "5" in why[ids["Cash"]][0]
      table = table_rows(bad, why)
      by_label = {r["label"].strip(): r for r in table}
      assert by_label["Cash"]["why"].startswith("Total current assets") and by_label["Assets"]["why"] == ""
      assert by_label["Total current assets"]["why"] != ""
  EOF

  new = '''def failed_rows(output: dict, cross_checks: List[dict] = None) -> dict:
      """Rows that belong to a FAILED check (the printed total and what it adds up), with why. Skipped and passing
      checks mark nothing. A failed cross-page check marks its totals on their own page."""
      why: dict = {}
      for check in output["checks"]:
          if check["status"] != "failed":
              continue
          text = (f"{check['label']} {check['period']}: printed {check['printed']}, adds up to {check['expected']}, "
                  f"off by {check['difference']} (allowed {check['bound']})")
          for row_id in check.get("row_ids", []):
              why.setdefault(row_id, []).append(text)
      for check in cross_checks or []:
          if check["status"] != "failed":
              continue
          text = (f"{check['label']} {check['period']}: printed {check['printed']}, expected {check['expected']}, "
                  f"off by {check['difference']} (allowed {check['bound']})")
          for ref in check.get("rows", []):
              if ref["page"] == output.get("page"):
                  why.setdefault(ref["row_id"], []).append(text)
      return why
  
  
  def table_rows(output: dict, why: dict = None) -> List[dict]:
      """One display row per table row: indented label, English reading label, role, the printed value per period,
      and, for rows in a failed check, why."""
      why = why or {}
      raw = {(r["id"].split("|")[0], r["period"]): r["raw"] for r in output["records"]}
      rows = []
      for row in output["rows"]:
          line = {"label": "\\u00a0\\u00a0\\u00a0" * row["level"] + row["label"], "label_en": row["label_en"] or "", "role": row["role"]}
          for period in output["periods"]:
              line[period] = raw.get((row["id"], period), "")
          line["why"] = " | ".join(why.get(row["id"], []))
          rows.append(line)
      return rows
  
  
  '''
  s = s.replace(old, new, 1)
  open(p, "w", encoding="utf-8").write(s)
  EOF

  def _app_for_file(path, patch=""):
      from streamlit.testing.v1 import AppTest
  
      def script(pdf_path, patch):
          import sys
          from pathlib import Path
  
          import streamlit as st
  
          sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parents[0]) if False else str(Path(pdf_path).parent))
          root = None
          for parent in Path(pdf_path).resolve().parents:
              if (parent / "app.py").exists():
                  root = parent
                  break
          sys.path.insert(0, str(root or Path(".").resolve()))
          import app
  
          class Upload:
              name = Path(pdf_path).name
  
              def getvalue(self):
                  return Path(pdf_path).read_bytes()
  
          st.file_uploader = lambda *args, **kwargs: Upload()
          if patch == "boom":
              def boom(*args, **kwargs):
                  raise RuntimeError("internal detail 12345")
              app.analyse_statement = boom
          app.main()
  
      return AppTest.from_function(script, args=(str(path), patch), default_timeout=90)

      """Unit, periods, the page image beside the table (rows of a failed check in red), checks and issues."""
      from bsparser.pages import render_page_png
  
      left, mid, right = st.columns(3)
      left.metric("Unit", f"{output['unit'] or 'not found'}", output["unit_raw"] or None)
      mid.metric("Periods", ", ".join(output["periods"]) or "not found")
      right.metric("Rows / records", f"{len(output['rows'])} / {len(output['records'])}")
  
      image_col, table_col = st.columns([2, 3])
      try:
          image_col.image(render_page_png(source, output["page"], dpi=90), caption=f"Page {output['page']} as printed",
                          use_container_width=True) if False else image_col.image(
              render_page_png(source, output["page"], dpi=90), caption=f"Page {output['page']} as printed", width="stretch")
      except ValueError:
          image_col.info("The page image could not be shown.")
      with table_col:
          if not output["rows"]:
              st.info(FRIENDLY["no_rows"])
          else:
              st.subheader("Table")
              if why:
                  st.caption("Rows in red belong to a failed check; the last column says why.")
              st.dataframe(styled_table(table_rows(output, why)), hide_index=True, width="stretch")
  
      st.subheader(f"Checks ({len(output['checks'])})")
      if output["checks"]:
          shown = pd.DataFrame(output["checks"]).drop(columns=["row_id", "row_ids"])
          shown.insert(0, "result", shown.pop("status").map({"pass": "pass", "failed": "FAILED", "skipped": "skipped"}))
          st.dataframe(shown, hide_index=True, width="stretch")
      else:
          st.info("No identity checks could be run on this page.")
  
      st.subheader(f"Issues ({len(output['issues'])})")
      if output["issues"]:
          st.dataframe(pd.DataFrame(output["issues"]), hide_index=True, width="stretch")
      else:
          st.write("None.")
  
  
  '''

          show_page(st, pd, first)
      else:

              st.markdown(f"### Page {output['page']}")

⏺ All five polish items are built and committed, and 185 tests pass. The one thing I could not do is look at the page in a real browser.
  
  1. **Only real balance sheets are offered.**
     - **Rule:** a page must have a balance-sheet title, an assets part and a total-assets line. On your four filings that offers 4098 page 6, 4224 page 7, 2452 page 6, and the bank's pages 5 and 14.
     - **Headings:** each offered page shows a heading, so the bank's consolidated and parent-only statements read apart.
     - **Escape:** if your page isn't listed, the "My page isn't listed" expander takes any page number.
  2. **Statement mode is on by default.** It already was. The checkbox starts ticked and a test checks it, so if you saw it off, that was an older version. I kept the checkbox so a standalone balance sheet can still be read alone.
  3. **Page image next to the table:** each page's image sits on the left of its table.
  4. **Failed-check rows are red.** The printed total and every row it adds up are highlighted, and a "why" column gives the check, the printed value, what it adds up to, the difference and the allowed bound. A failed cross-page check marks its totals on their own page. Skipped and passing checks mark nothing. To make this possible, every check now lists the rows it used (`row_ids`), and the cross-page checks list the page and row of each total.
  5. **Friendly messages, no red errors or tracebacks:**
     - **Covered cases:** not a PDF, a scanned PDF with no text layer, a document with no balance sheet page, no table on the page, and an unexpected failure.
     - **Wording:** each message uses plain words and says what to do next. The technical detail sits in a collapsed expander. The status texts have a next step too.
  
  **What I have not verified:** I tried to drive the app in a real browser, but the upload tool failed twice with the same error, so I stopped and closed the tab and the server. Streamlit's test harness covers the structure: images rendered per page, the "why" data on failed rows, and the friendly messages. It cannot show colours or how wide the image column is. Please give the page one look, especially the red rows with `round` selected on 4098 (page 6 then fails current assets by 5).
  
  My slips this round:
  - **Red commit:** I committed once while one test was still red, because the `tail` in my command hid the exit code. I fixed it in the next commit and now check the real exit code.
  - **Test isolation:** my own "unexpected failure" test left a patch on the shared app module, which broke later tests until I restored it after each run.
  - **Test-writing mistakes, corrected:** several wrong test assumptions (an id, a CSS rule count, a row count) and five tests written at once.
  - **Assertion updated:** one old assertion changed because the friendlier status text replaced `ok:`.
  - **D22 added late:** it was referenced in a commit message before it was written into `docs/decisions.md`, and it's there now.
  
  I'd suggest your look at the page next. After that the real gap is still a fresh set of filings, ideally one English and one scanned. Tell me which you want.

  old = s[s.index("out_dir = Path(sys.argv[1])"):s.index("    json_path, _ = write_statement_outputs(statement, out_dir)")]
  new = '''out_dir = Path(sys.argv[1])
  in_dir = Path(sys.argv[2]) if len(sys.argv) > 2 else Path("data/heldout")
  for pdf in sorted(in_dir.glob("*.pdf")):
      try:
          statement = analyse_statement(pdf)
      except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 - a crash is a result, shown and recorded, never hidden
          trace = traceback.format_exc().strip().splitlines()
          print("=" * 100)
          print(f"{pdf.name}: CRASH {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}")
          print("   ", trace[-3].strip())
          print("   ", trace[-2].strip()[:150])
          continue
  '''
  s = s.replace(old, new, 1)
  open(p, "w", encoding="utf-8").write(s)
  EOF

⏺ Bash(.venv/bin/python -I - <<'EOF'
  import sys, traceback
  sys.path.insert(0, "src")
  import pdfplumber
  f = "data/fresh/vg1mvt19a00h4bsv.pdf"
  def attempt(name, fn):
      try:
          r = fn(); print(f"OK    {name}: {r}")
      except Exception as e:
          tb = traceback.extract_tb(e.__traceback__)[-1]
          print(f"FAIL  {name}: {type(e).__name__}: {str(e)[:70]} (at {tb.name}, {tb.filename.split('/')[-1]}:{tb.lineno})")
  attempt("open + page count", lambda: len(pdfplumber.open(f).pages))
  def all_text():
      with pdfplumber.open(f) as pdf:
          return sum(1 for p in pdf.pages if (p.extract_text() or "").strip())
  attempt("open + extract_text on every page", all_text)
  def each():
      out = []
      with pdfplumber.open(f) as pdf:
          for n, p in enumerate(pdf.pages, 1):
              try: p.extract_text()
              except Exception as e: out.append((n, type(e).__name__, str(e)[:40]))
      return out
  attempt("per-page extract_text errors", each)
  from bsparser.pages import find_balance_sheet_pages, extract_page
  attempt("strict finder", lambda: [(c.page, c.heading[:40]) for c in find_balance_sheet_pages(f)])
  EOF)

⏺ Bash(python3 - <<'EOF'
  p = "docs/report.md"
  s = open(p, encoding="utf-8").read()
  marker = "## Why the baseline loses on repeated labels"
  section = """## Fresh filings: the real held-out test
  
  Three filings the parser had never seen (`data/fresh/`) were run once each, as is, with no fixes, through the same path as the page and the command line (`scripts/run_heldout.py <out_dir> data/fresh`, `analyse_statement`, nothing picked by hand). The parser is deterministic, so a rerun gives the same result. Raw results for the runs that produced output: `outputs/fresh_run/`. This is the first test where nothing was built against the files, and the result is mixed: one of three works, two do not.
  
  | file | what it is | pages read | result | what happened |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | 140120260508520388.pdf | GMS Inc. (544A), Japanese 決算短信, million yen | 8, 9 | **ok** | Page 8: 20 of 20 checks pass. Page 9: 14 of 14 pass. Cross-page checks 4 of 4 pass (total assets equals total liabilities and net assets exactly: 86,479 and 85,763). Rounding `cut`, taken from the cover sentence (百万円未満切捨て). Only informational issues (the page number lines). No gold: this shows the totals hold, not that every cell is right. |
  | ir_202603tanshin-en.pdf | Yokogawa Electric (6841), English tanshin, millions of yen | 9, 10 | **unreadable**, no checks run | The finder chose the right pages (page 9 is the consolidated balance sheet, page 10 its continuation), but the header is not understood. See causes below. Page 9: 31 column-count issues, 18 irregular-indent issues, unit missing. Page 10: 33, 28, unit missing. |
  | vg1mvt19a00h4bsv.pdf | Shimadzu (7701), English translation, AES-encrypted | 15 chosen | **crash** | `PdfminerException: Invalid IV size (2) for CBC` raised when the parser opens the one-page file that `pdfseparate` cuts out of the encrypted PDF (see causes below). The page shows "Something went wrong while reading page 15" (the friendly path); the script and command line show the raw exception. |
  
  Causes, each checked directly and none fixed:
  - **Yokogawa, three things at the header.**
    1. On the date line `As of March 31, 2025   As of March 31, 2026`, the last tokens `31,` and `2026` are read as amounts, so the second date is cut off the label. Only one date is left in the label, and the rule that finds the end of the header needs two dates in one line. It finds none, so zero header lines are found and the whole page, title included, is read as table rows.
    2. `(Millions of yen)` is not recognised as a unit. The parser knows `in millions of yen`.
    3. Without periods, every valued row is a column-count mismatch, no check can run, and the status is `unreadable`.
  - **Shimadzu, the encrypted file.** The original opens in pdfplumber and its text reads fine, and the finder picks page 15 correctly. The crash happens later: the one-page file cut out by `pdfseparate` is still marked encrypted, poppler reads it, and pdfminer fails on it. The other English file is also encrypted (AES-256, copying allowed) and works, so encryption alone is not the cause. Why pdfminer fails on this particular cut page is not established.
  - **Finder, a weakness on English filings.** For Shimadzu the strict rule offers pages 1, 3 and 15. Pages 1 and 3 are summary tables titled "financial position" with a "Total assets" line, not balance sheets. The real page is ranked first, so the default is right, but the list has false entries.
  
  What this shows and does not show:
  - **It is the honest generalisation number so far:** 1 of 3 unseen filings parses end to end. The Japanese filing in the same family as the development set passes. The two English filings, the first real English ones, do not.
  - **No gold for these files.** The Japanese `ok` means every identity and cross-page check holds. It is not a cell accuracy.
  - **Three files cannot give a rate.** Both failures are about English formatting and an encrypted file, which the development files never contained. They say what to look at next, not how often it will happen.
  
  """
  assert marker in s
  s = s.replace(marker, section + marker, 1)
  old = "- **Everything measured so far is in-sample.**"
  assert old in s
  s = s.replace(old, "- **The fresh filings are the only held-out result, and it is mixed (1 of 3).** Everything else measured so far is in-sample.", 1)
  open(p, "w", encoding="utf-8").write(s)
  
  p = "README.md"
  r = open(p, encoding="utf-8").read()
  r = r.replace("The four held-out filings exposed gaps that were fixed test first; they now pass end to end (all checks and cross-page checks), but they are development data now, not a held-out result.", "The four held-out filings exposed gaps that were fixed test first; they now pass end to end (all checks and cross-page checks), but they are development data now, not a held-out result. The only true held-out test is three fresh filings, run once with no fixes: 1 of 3 works (a Japanese filing), the two English ones do not (header not understood; an encrypted PDF crashes the page-cutting step). Details and causes in `docs/report.md`.", 1)
  open(p, "w", encoding="utf-8").write(r)
  EOF
  grep -c "—" docs/report.md README.md; .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=short > "$TMPDIR/full.txt" 2>&1; code=$?; echo "full suite exit code: $code"; tail -1 "$TMPDIR/full.txt")

  ⎿  ?? data/fresh/
     b9575e9 docs: fresh filings, the real held-out test (3 files run once as is: 1 ok, 1 unreadable, 1 crash), with causes; runner takes an input folder and records crashes

⏺ The three fresh filings were each run once, as is, and the results are in `docs/report.md` as the real held-out test. Nothing in the parser changed. The result is mixed: 1 of 3 works.
  
  | file | result | what happened |
  |---|---|---|
  | GMS Inc., Japanese, million yen | **ok** | Pages 8 and 9 were read. 34 of 34 page checks and 4 of 4 cross-page checks pass. Rounding `cut` was taken from the cover sentence. |
  | Yokogawa, English, millions of yen | **unreadable** | The right pages were chosen (9 and 10), but the header isn't understood, so no check could run. |
  | Shimadzu, English translation, AES-encrypted | **crash** | The page shows "Something went wrong while reading page 15". The script shows the raw exception. |
  
  **Causes**, each checked directly:
  - **Yokogawa:**
    - **Date line:** on `As of March 31, 2025   As of March 31, 2026`, the last tokens `31,` and `2026` are read as amounts. The header end is never found, so the whole page, title included, is read as table rows.
    - **Unit wording:** `(Millions of yen)` isn't recognised, because the parser knows `in millions of yen`.
    - **No periods:** without periods, every row is a column-count mismatch.
  - **Shimadzu:** my first guess was wrong. The original file opens fine and the right page 15 is found. The crash comes when the parser opens the one-page file that `pdfseparate` cuts out of the encrypted PDF. That cut file is still marked encrypted, poppler reads it, and pdfminer fails on it (`Invalid IV size (2) for CBC`). The other English file is encrypted too and works, so encryption alone isn't the reason. Why pdfminer fails on this one cut page isn't established.
  - **Finder:** for Shimadzu it also offers pages 1 and 3, which are "financial position" summary tables, not balance sheets. The real page ranks first, so the default is right, but the strict rule isn't airtight on English filings.
  
  Three files can't give a rate. The Japanese filing in the family I developed on passes. The two English filings, the first real English ones, don't, and both failures come from things the development files never contained. They show what to look at next, not how often it will happen.
  
  `data/fresh/` is still untracked. I haven't committed the three PDFs, because you didn't ask for it and they're your submission's content. Say the word and I'll commit them, as we did for the held-out set.
  
  Whenever you're ready, I suggest we decide fixes one at a time, with these first:
  1. the English date line and the unit wording (the Yokogawa failure)

      """`As of March 31, 2025  As of March 31, 2026`: the last two tokens (31, and 2026) look like amounts but finish
      a date. Move just enough leading amount tokens back into the label to complete the date. A row such as
      `Balance at March 31, 2025  100  200` keeps its amounts, because they add no date."""
      label_text = " ".join(w["text"] for w in row[:split])
      dates = len(find_periods(label_text))
      for k in range(1, len(row) - split + 1):
          if len(find_periods(label_text + " " + " ".join(w["text"] for w in row[split:split + k]))) > dates:

      return split

  def test_english_unit_wording_without_in_is_recognised_and_kept_as_printed():

  def test_a_page_can_be_read_straight_from_the_original_file_without_cutting_a_new_pdf():
      import tempfile
  
      from bsparser.pages import extract_page
      from bsparser.pdf_lines import extract_lines
  
      bank = Path(__file__).parent.parent / "data" / "heldout" / "tanshin202603.pdf"
      direct = extract_lines(bank, page=5)
      cut = extract_lines(extract_page(bank, 5, Path(tempfile.mkdtemp()) / "p.pdf"))
      assert [(l.label, l.amounts) for l in direct] == [(l.label, l.amounts) for l in cut]
      assert any(l.label == "資産の部合計" for l in direct)
  EOF

  p = "src/bsparser/pdf_lines.py"
  s = open(p, encoding="utf-8").read()
  old = '''def extract_lines(pdf_path) -> List[Line]:
      with pdfplumber.open(pdf_path) as pdf:
          words = pdf.pages[0].extract_words()
      return lines_from_words(words)'''
  new = '''def extract_lines(pdf_path, page: int = 1) -> List[Line]:
      """Lines of one page (1-based), read straight from the file. No one-page copy is cut out first: cutting a page
      out of an encrypted PDF left a file pdfminer could not read."""
      with pdfplumber.open(pdf_path) as pdf:
          words = pdf.pages[page - 1].extract_words()
      return lines_from_words(words)'''
  assert old in s
  open(p, "w", encoding="utf-8").write(s.replace(old, new, 1))

      return parse_lines(extract_lines(pdf_path))'''
  new = '''def parse_page(pdf_path, page: int = 1) -> Page:
      return parse_lines(extract_lines(pdf_path, page))'''
  assert old in s
  open(p, "w", encoding="utf-8").write(s.replace(old, new, 1))

  import pytest
  
  
  @pytest.mark.parametrize("sentence,unit,mode", [
      ("Amounts less than one million yen are truncated.", "million_yen", "cut"),
      ("Figures are rounded down to the nearest million yen.", "million_yen", "cut"),
      ("Amounts of less than one thousand yen are discarded.", "thousand_yen", "cut"),
      ("(Any amount less than one\nmillion yen is disregarded.)", "million_yen", "cut"),  # broken across a line
      ("Ratios are rounded down to one decimal place.", "million_yen", "unstated"),  # not an amount cut-off
      ("Amounts are rounded to the nearest million yen.", "million_yen", "unstated"),  # rounding, not a cut-off: not read as cut
  ])
  def test_english_cut_off_wording_variants_and_things_that_must_not_count(tmp_path, sentence, unit, mode):
      from reportlab.lib.pagesizes import A4
      from reportlab.pdfgen import canvas

      c = canvas.Canvas(str(path), pagesize=A4)
      y = 780
      for line in sentence.split("\n"):
          c.drawString(60, y, line)
          y -= 14
      c.save()
      assert find_rounding_hint(path, unit).mode == mode

  p = "src/bsparser/rounding.py"

  ENGLISH_UNIT_OF = {"million": "million_yen", "thousand": "thousand_yen"}
  CUT_VERB = r"(?:disregarded|truncated|discarded|omitted|ignored|cut off|rounded down)"
  # "any amount less than one million yen is disregarded" / "amounts below one thousand yen are truncated"
  ENGLISH_LESS_THAN = re.compile(r"(?:less than|below|under) (?:one )?(million|thousand) yen[^.]{0,40}?" + CUT_VERB)
  # "yen amounts are rounded down to millions" / "truncated to the nearest thousand yen"
  ENGLISH_DOWN_TO = re.compile(r"(?:rounded down|truncated|cut off) to (?:the nearest )?(?:one )?(million|thousand)s?(?: of)?(?: yen)?")''', 1)

      english = " ".join(text.lower().split())  # one line, so a sentence broken across lines still matches
      for pattern in (ENGLISH_LESS_THAN, ENGLISH_DOWN_TO):
          found += [(m.group(0), ENGLISH_UNIT_OF[m.group(1)]) for m in pattern.finditer(english)]'''
  assert old in s
  s = s.replace(old, new, 1)
  open(p, "w", encoding="utf-8").write(s)

  from bsparser.page import parse_lines
  from bsparser.pdf_lines import Line
  
  HEADER = [Line("（単位：百万円）", 400.0, 70.0), Line("(2025年３月31日) (2026年３月31日)", 300.0, 94.0)]
  LONG = "Notes and accounts receivable - trade, and contract"
  
  
  def page(body):
      return parse_lines(HEADER + [Line("Assets", 60.0, 120.0), Line("Current assets", 72.0, 134.0),
                                   Line("Cash and deposits", 84.0, 148.0, ["100", "100"])] + body)
  
  
  def test_numbers_sitting_between_the_two_lines_of_a_wrapped_label_are_attached_to_the_label():
      p = page([Line(LONG, 84.0, 162.0), Line("", 400.0, 168.0, ["500", "600"]), Line("assets", 84.0, 174.0),
                Line("Inventories", 84.0, 188.0, ["70", "80"]), Line("Total current assets", 84.0, 202.0, ["670", "780"])])
      merged = [r for r in p.rows if r.label == LONG + " assets"]
      assert len(merged) == 1 and [a.value for a in merged[0].values] == [500, 600] and merged[0].role == "item"
      assert [(i.kind, i.row_id) for i in p.issues] == [("wrapped_label_merged", merged[0].id)]  # no ignored_line left
      assert not [r for r in p.rows if r.label in (LONG, "assets")]  # the two halves are not separate empty headers
  
  
  def test_numbers_just_below_a_single_label_line_are_attached_to_it():
      p = page([Line("Long label with its numbers a little lower", 84.0, 162.0), Line("", 400.0, 168.0, ["500", "600"]),
                Line("Inventories", 84.0, 188.0, ["70", "80"]), Line("Total current assets", 84.0, 202.0, ["670", "780"])])
      row = [r for r in p.rows if r.label == "Long label with its numbers a little lower"][0]
      assert [a.value for a in row.values] == [500, 600]
      assert [i.kind for i in p.issues] == ["wrapped_label_merged"]
  
  
  def test_a_real_page_number_keeps_its_wording_and_other_stray_numbers_say_they_were_not_attached():
      pn = page([Line("Inventories", 84.0, 162.0, ["70", "80"]), Line("", 285.0, 800.0, ["-", "11", "-"])])
      assert [i.kind for i in pn.issues] == ["ignored_line"] and "looks like a page number" in pn.issues[0].message
      stray = page([Line("Inventories", 84.0, 162.0, ["70", "80"]), Line("", 400.0, 300.0, ["149,127", "156,326"])])
      assert [i.kind for i in stray.issues] == ["ignored_line"]
      assert "not attached to any row" in stray.issues[0].message and "149,127" in stray.issues[0].message
      assert "page number" not in stray.issues[0].message
  EOF
  .venv/bin/python -m pytest -q --tb=line tests/test_orphan_numbers.py > "$TMPDIR/t.txt" 2>&1; echo "exit $?"; tail -4 "$TMPDIR/t.txt" | cut -c1-170
  python3 - <<'EOF'
  p = "src/bsparser/page.py"
  s = open(p, encoding="utf-8").read()
  s = s.replace("def parse_lines(lines: List[Line]) -> Page:", '''def _looks_like_page_number(amounts: List[str]) -> bool:
      return bool(PAGE_NUMBER.match(unicodedata.normalize("NFKC", "".join(amounts)).replace(" ", "")))
  
  
  def _attach_orphan_numbers(lines: List[Line]):
      """A wrapped label can print its numbers on a line of their own, between the two label lines (a label line
      with no values, a numbers line with no label 6 points below it, the label's continuation 6 points further).
      Attach the numbers to the label (both halves joined), or to the single label line just above them. Only with
      tight spacing, and never for something that looks like a page number. Returns (lines, merged labels)."""
      gaps = sorted(b.top - a.top for a, b in zip(lines, lines[1:]))
      usual = gaps[len(gaps) // 2] if gaps else 0.0
  
      def tight(a: Line, b: Line) -> bool:
          return b.top - a.top < TIGHT_RATIO * usual
  
      out: List[Line] = []
      merged: List[str] = []
      i = 0
      while i < len(lines):
          line = lines[i]
          nxt = lines[i + 1] if i + 1 < len(lines) else None
          after = lines[i + 2] if i + 2 < len(lines) else None
          if (line.label and not line.amounts and nxt is not None and not nxt.label and nxt.amounts
                  and not _looks_like_page_number(nxt.amounts) and tight(line, nxt)):
              if (after is not None and after.label and not after.amounts and tight(nxt, after)
                      and abs(after.x0 - line.x0) <= LEVEL_TOLERANCE):
                  attached = Line(_join(line.label, after.label), line.x0, line.top, nxt.amounts)
                  i += 3
              else:
                  attached = Line(line.label, line.x0, line.top, nxt.amounts)
                  i += 2
              out.append(attached)
              merged.append(attached.label)
              continue
          out.append(line)
          i += 1
      return out, merged
  
  
  def parse_lines(lines: List[Line]) -> Page:''', 1)
  old = '''    header, table = _split_header(lines)
      ignored = [line for line in table if not line.label]'''
  new = '''    header, table = _split_header(lines)
      table, attached_labels = _attach_orphan_numbers(table)
      ignored = [line for line in table if not line.label]'''
  assert old in s
  s = s.replace(old, new, 1)
  old = '''    for line in ignored:
          page.issues.append(Issue("ignored_line", "", f"line without a label ignored (page number?): amounts {line.amounts}"))'''
  new = '''    for label in attached_labels:
          row = next(r for r in page.rows if r.label == label)
          page.issues.append(Issue("wrapped_label_merged", row.id,
                                   f"{label!r}: its numbers were printed on a line of their own and were attached to it"))
      for line in ignored:
          if _looks_like_page_number(line.amounts):
              message = f"line without a label ignored: amounts {line.amounts} look like a page number"
          else:
              message = f"numbers without a label were not attached to any row and are left out of the table: amounts {line.amounts}"
          page.issues.append(Issue("ignored_line", "", message))'''
  assert old in s
  s = s.replace(old, new, 1)
  open(p, "w", encoding="utf-8").write(s)
  EOF

A cover that says amounts are rounded (to the nearest), or that uses other words, gives `unstated`: the strict bound is used and a cut-off filing can then show a false failure. The mode can always be chosen by the user.
  - **Wrapped labels:** the numbers-on-their-own-line rule needs tight spacing (under 0.7 of the usual line pitch) and the same indent. Other arrangements, for example numbers above both label lines, are not handled: the numbers are then left out of the table, listed in an `ignored_line` issue, and the affected sum check fails.
  - **Statement assembly is heuristic:** the assets page plus up to 2 following pages with liabilities or net assets wording and the same unit and periods. A statement spread wider, or a layout with liabilities before assets, is not joined.
  - **The finder can still offer a page that is not a balance sheet** on layouts we have not seen (it needs a title, an assets part and a total-assets line). The real page has ranked first in every file so far, and "My page isn't listed" covers a miss.
  - **Not read at all:** the statement of changes in equity (reported `unreadable`, no crash), scanned pages (the vision fallback is a prototype and is not wired into the page or the statement path), merged cells, other eras than Reiwa, and any statement that is not a balance sheet.
  - **Language and company coverage:** all real filings tested are Japanese or English 決算短信 of listed companies reporting in thousands or millions of yen.
  """

     def analyse(pdf_path, page: Optional[int] = None, rounding: str = "auto") -> Dict:
         """One path for the command line, the held-out script and the page, so no result depends on hand-picked options.
         Finds the balance sheet page (unless given), parses it with the parser unchanged, and sets the rounding mode:
         `auto` takes the cover hint (D18), an explicit value is recorded as chosen by the user."""
         with pdfplumber.open(pdf_path) as pdf:
             pages_total = len(pdf.pages)
         if page is None:
             candidates = find_balance_sheet_pages(pdf_path) or find_balance_sheet_pages(pdf_path, strict=False)
             page = candidates[0].page if candidates else 1
         parsed = _parse(pdf_path, page)
         mode, source, sentence, note = _resolve_rounding(pdf_path, parsed.unit, rounding)
         return _page_output(pdf_path, page, pages_total, mode, source, sentence, note, parsed=parsed)
     
     
     CONTINUES = re.compile(r"負債の部|純資産の部|負債合計|純資産合計|負債純資産合計|total liabilities|total net assets|total equity", re.I)
     MAX_FOLLOWING = 2
     RANK = {"ok": 0, "failed": 1, "unreadable": 2}
     
     
     def analyse_statement(pdf_path, page: Optional[int] = None, rounding: str = "auto") -> Dict:
         """Q23. The assets page plus the following pages (up to 2) with the same unit and periods that carry liabilities
         or net assets wording, each parsed page by page with the parser unchanged, then checked across pages.
         The rounding mode is resolved once, from the assets page, so every page uses the same one."""
         with pdfplumber.open(pdf_path) as pdf:
             pages_total = len(pdf.pages)
             texts = {n: pdf.pages[n - 1].extract_text() or "" for n in range(1, pages_total + 1)}
         if page is None:
             candidates = find_balance_sheet_pages(pdf_path) or find_balance_sheet_pages(pdf_path, strict=False)
             page = candidates[0].page if candidates else 1
         first = _parse(pdf_path, page)
         mode, source, sentence, note = _resolve_rounding(pdf_path, first.unit, rounding)
         outputs = [_page_output(pdf_path, page, pages_total, mode, source, sentence, note, parsed=first)]
         for nxt in range(page + 1, min(page + MAX_FOLLOWING, pages_total) + 1):
             if not CONTINUES.search(texts[nxt]):
                 break
             parsed = _parse(pdf_path, nxt)
             if (parsed.unit, parsed.periods) != (first.unit, first.periods):
                 break
             outputs.append(_page_output(pdf_path, nxt, pages_total, mode, source, sentence, note, parsed=parsed))
         cross = cross_checks(outputs, mode)
         worst = max([o["status"] for o in outputs] + (["failed"] if any(c["status"] == "failed" for c in cross) else []),
                     key=RANK.get)
         return {"source": str(pdf_path), "status": worst, "rounding": mode, "rounding_source": source,
                 "rounding_sentence": sentence, "rounding_note": note, "pages": outputs, "cross_checks": cross}
     from typing import Dict
     
     from bsparser.arbitrate import arbitrate
     from bsparser.output import build_output, output_from_page
     from bsparser.page import parse_lines
     from bsparser.vision import _claude_runner, lines_from_vision, run_vision
     
     
     def parse_with_fallback(pdf_path, runner=_claude_runner) -> Dict:
         """D7 routing. Text first. Only a page with no usable text, or whose identity checks fail, goes to vision.
         The same checks judge both outputs."""
         text = build_output(pdf_path)
         needs_vision = not text["records"] or text["status"] != "ok"
         if not needs_vision:
             text["extractor"] = "text"
             return text
         reply = run_vision(pdf_path, runner=runner)
         vision = output_from_page(parse_lines(lines_from_vision(reply)), str(pdf_path))
         return arbitrate(text=text, vision=vision)

     src/bsparser/vision.py:23:def extract_reply(text: str) -> Dict:

     src/bsparser/vision.py:61:def run_vision(pdf_path, runner=_claude_runner, dpi: int = 150) -> Dict:

     src/bsparser/arbitrate.py:32:def arbitrate(text: Dict, vision: Dict) -> Dict:

⏺ Bash(cat > docs/architecture.md <<'EOF'
  # Architecture
  
  Two diagrams: what runs when you parse a PDF, and how the result is judged. Module names are the files in `src/bsparser/`. Solid boxes are wired in and run. **Dashed orange boxes are designed and tested but not wired into the command line or the app** (see the legend).
  
  ## 1. Parsing flow
  
  ```mermaid
  flowchart TD
      PDF["PDF in<br/>(CLI, app.py upload, scripts)"]
      PDF --> OPEN{"Has a text layer?"}
      OPEN -- "app only: no text" --> SCANMSG["Friendly message:<br/>looks scanned, not read"]
      OPEN -- yes --> FIND
  
      subgraph PAGES["Pick and join pages (analyse, pages, statement)"]
          FIND["Find balance sheet pages<br/>pages.find_balance_sheet_pages<br/>strict first, then relaxed<br/>user can override the page"]
          FIND --> CUT["Cut each page from the original file<br/>pages.extract_page"]
          CUT --> JOIN["Join: assets page + up to 2 following pages<br/>with liabilities or net assets wording,<br/>same unit and periods<br/>analyse.analyse_statement"]
      end
  
      subgraph READ["Read one page (page.parse_page)"]
          LINES["Text lines with positions<br/>pdf_lines.extract_lines"]
          META["Unit: units.find_unit<br/>Dates / periods: periods.find_periods<br/>Rounding: rounding.find_rounding_hint<br/>(cover sentence, or user choice, or unstated)"]
          AMT["Amount tokens<br/>amounts: nil is None, triangle is negative,<br/>triangle 0 is 0"]
          ROWS["Build rows<br/>labels, wrapped labels (wrapped),<br/>indent levels (levels)"]
          SEC["Section path<br/>and roles: header, item, subtotal, total,<br/>contra, net (roles, totals)"]
          LINES --> META
          LINES --> AMT
          AMT --> ROWS --> SEC
          META --> SEC
      end
  
      CUT --> LINES
      JOIN --> LINES
  
      SEC --> REC["Records: one per row and period<br/>records.make_records<br/>English label from labels.english_label"]
      SEC --> CHK["Identity checks (checks.run_checks)<br/>items sum to subtotal, gross + depreciation = net,<br/>current + fixed = total assets<br/>tolerance set by the rounding mode"]
      JOIN --> XCHK["Cross-page checks (statement.cross_checks)<br/>total assets = liabilities + net assets"]
      CHK --> STATUS["Status per page: ok / failed / unreadable<br/>(status.page_status)<br/>worst of pages and cross checks"]
      XCHK --> STATUS
      REC --> OUT
      STATUS --> OUT
  
      subgraph OUTPUTS["Outputs (output)"]
          OUT["build_output / write_outputs"]
          OUT --> JSON["name.json<br/>source of truth: status, unit, periods,<br/>rounding, issues, checks, rows, records"]
          OUT --> CSV["name.records.csv"]
          OUT --> STJ["name.statement.json and<br/>.statement.records.csv (multi-page)"]
      end
  
      JSON --> APP["Streamlit app (app.py)<br/>table with page image, red failed rows with why,<br/>checks, issues, CSV and JSON download"]
      STJ --> APP
  
      subgraph VISION["Scanned pages: vision fallback (prototype)"]
          direction TB
          PIPE["pipeline.parse_with_fallback<br/>text first; vision only if no records<br/>or status is not ok"]
          RUN["vision.run_vision<br/>render page, ask headless Claude,<br/>lines_from_vision"]
          ARB["arbitrate: same checks judge<br/>text and vision outputs, disagreements flagged"]
          PIPE --> RUN --> ARB
      end
      PDF -.-> PIPE
      ARB -.-> OUT
  
      classDef notwired fill:#fff4e5,stroke:#e67e22,stroke-dasharray: 5 4,color:#333;
      class PIPE,RUN,ARB,SCANMSG notwired;
  ```
  
  ### Legend and what is not wired in
  
  - **Solid boxes** are called by `python -m bsparser`, `scripts/run_heldout.py` and `app.py` (through `analyse` or `analyse_statement`).
  - **Dashed orange boxes** are not on that path:
    - **Vision fallback** (`vision`, `arbitrate`, `pipeline`). Tested, and run for real three times on the original page (`outputs/vision_*`), but nothing in the CLI, the scripts or the app calls `pipeline.parse_with_fallback`. A scanned PDF therefore stops at the app message, or yields no records on the command line. Prototype only.
    - **Scan message** is in the app only. The command line does not check for a text layer first.
  - Statement mode (page joining and cross-page checks) is on by default in the app and behind `--statement` on the command line. Without it, one page is parsed on its own.
  - Not read at all: equity statements (reported `unreadable`), merged cells, eras other than Reiwa.
  
  ## 2. Evaluation side
  
  This side does not run when you parse a file. It judges the parser's output after the fact.
  
  ```mermaid
  flowchart LR
      subgraph INPUT["Inputs"]
          PAGE["Real page<br/>data/balance_sheet_after_correction.pdf"]
          GOLDT["Gold answers<br/>data/gold/*.template.md<br/>37 rows x 2 periods = 74 cells"]
          FIX["English fixtures (clean, messy)<br/>made by tests/make_english_fixture.py"]
          UNSEEN["Held-out filings (4, now dev data)<br/>Fresh filings (3, not unseen anymore)<br/>no gold, only status and checks"]
      end
  
      PAGE --> PARSER["Parser<br/>build_output / analyse_statement"]
      FIX --> PARSER
      UNSEEN --> PARSER
      PAGE --> BASE["Baseline: first match per label<br/>baseline.first_match"]
      GOLDT --> GLD["gold.load_gold / evaluate"]
  
      PARSER --> GLD
      BASE --> GLD
      GLD --> ACC["accuracy + report<br/>cells exact, misses by kind<br/>parser 74/74, baseline 60/74"]
  
      PARSER --> IDC["Identity checks<br/>20 of 20 pass on the real page"]
      UNSEEN --> RUNS["scripts/run_heldout.py<br/>outputs/heldout_before, heldout_after,<br/>fresh_run, fresh_after"]
      PARSER --> RUNS
  
      PARSER --> REV["Reviewer session (separate Claude)<br/>reads the page image, not the code<br/>docs/label-check.md, translation-check.md,<br/>final-review.md, final-review-2.md"]
  
      ACC --> REPORT["docs/report.md<br/>results, limits, known limits (code frozen)"]
      IDC --> REPORT
      RUNS --> REPORT
      REV --> REPORT
      DEC["docs/decisions.md D1 to D23"] --> REPORT
  
      VIS["Vision runs (3 real, headless Claude)<br/>vision_eval.judge_replies<br/>outputs/vision_*"]
      PAGE -.-> VIS
      VIS -.-> REPORT
  
      classDef notwired fill:#fff4e5,stroke:#e67e22,stroke-dasharray: 5 4,color:#333;
      class VIS notwired;
  ```
  
  ### What each piece is worth
  
  - **Gold** was read by a separate Claude chat that had seen the page text before, so it is not independent. Nobody who reads Japanese checked the labels.
  - **Baseline** is the naive first match by label. It loses 14 cells, all on repeated labels, which is why the parser keeps the section path.
  - **Identity checks** run inside the parser and need no gold, so they are the only signal on files without gold.
  - **Reviewer session** compared the final records with the page image cell by cell, twice: 74 of 74 match. Its one role difference (r12 貸倒引当金) is unexplained and contradicted by the gold template, see the report.
  - **Held-out and fresh filings** have no gold. They show status and checks only. The held-out four are development data after their fixes, the fresh three too after the fixes that followed.
